246.	avi tām,
	puṇariya ṉiliyiṭip poruḷnilik kuta1navum
	viṉiceyaṉ maruṅkiṉ kālamoṭu varunavum
	vēṟṟumip poruḷvayiṉ urupā kunavum
	acinilik kiḷavi āki varunavum
	iciniṟik kiḷavi āki varunavum
	tattam kuṟippiṉ poruḷcey kunavum
	oppil vaḻiyāṉ poruḷcey kunavumeṉ
	ṟappaṇ piṉavē nuvaluṅ kāli.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, av iṭic coṟkaḷiṉ pākupāṭu āmāṟu uṇarttutal nutaliṟṟu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- mēl iṭiccol eṉṟu 2collap paṭṭavitām, irumoḻi tammiṟ puṇartal iyaṉṟa nilimik kaṇ avaṟṟiṉ poruḷnilimikku utaviceytu varuvaṉavum, viṉiccoṟkaḷi muṭikkumiṭattuk kālam kāṭṭum iṭic coṟkaḷōṭu kūṭit 3tāmpālkāṭṭum coṟkaḷāy varuvaṉavum, vēṟupāṭu ceyta poruṇmiyiṭattu vēṟṟumi yurupāy varuvaṉavum, vaḻakkiṉkaṇ tamakku ōr poruḷaṉṟit tām aṭinta peyarviṉikaḷi 4aṭiyappaṇṇi niṟkum 5nilimiyavāy 6varuvaṉavum, ceyyuḷiṭattup poruḷiṉṟi ōrōr 7ōciyi niṟittaṟ poruṇmiyavāy varuvaṉavum, peyarviṉikaḷpōla viḷaṅkap poruḷuṇarttāta 8coṟkaḷ tattaṅ kuṟippāṉē oru poruḷi uṇartti varuvaṉavum 9ottal eṉṉum vāypāṭu 10taṉkaṇṇillāk kūṟṟāṉē 11niṉṟa oppumip poruṇmiyi uṇarttivaruvaṉavum eṉṟu collappaṭṭa avvilakkaṇaṅkaḷi uṭiyaṉavām; avaṟṟic collumkālattu eṉṟavāṟu.
12(u-m.) 
puṇarccikkaṇ varuvaṉa eḻuttōttiṉuḷ 13iṉṉē vaṟṟē eṉṉum cūttirattāṉ ōtappaṭṭaṉa.
iṉit taṉṉiṉamuṭittal eṉpataṉāl kāram, karam, kāṉ eṉṉum eḻuttuc cāriyiyum koḷḷappaṭum. 
viṉic 14coṟkaḷ pālkāṭṭi varuvaṉa, viṉiyiyaluḷ, 15am ām mutalāka  īṟupaṟṟi 16ōtiṉa ellām eṉak koḷka.
iṉi, av viṉic coṟkaḷ kālamoṭu varuvaṉavum eṉṟataṉāṟ kālamkāṭṭi varuvaṉa, uṇṭāṉ eṉpuḻi iṭikkaṭ ṭakaramum, uṇṇā niṉṟāṉ eṉpuḻi 17niṉṟum, uṇpāṉ eṉpuḻip pakaramum, attaṉmiya piṟavum eṉak koḷka. 
kālam uṇartti varutalāṉ avi kālam eṉappaṭṭaṉa.
uṇṭāṉ eṉpuḻik kālaṅkāṭṭum ṭakarattuṭaṉē pāl kāṭṭum 18āṉ kūṭa vantavāṟu aṟika.
ivvāṟu pālum kālamum kāṭṭuvaṉa viṉikku eṉṟu  19otiṉaṉēlum, ataṉip perumpāṉmiyākki uṇṭavaṉ eṉṟāṟpōlap peyarkkum koḷka.
iṉi, oṉṟeṉa muṭittal eṉpataṉāl 20viṉikkaṭ pāl uṇarttuvaṉavēyaṉṟip peyarkkēuriya vāypāṭṭāl uṇarttuvaṉavum koḷḷappaṭum.
avi nampi, naṅki eṉṉum ikara aikāra 21īṟṟuppōlvaṉa 22eṉak koḷka. vēṟṟumiyurupāy varuvaṉa vēṟṟumiyōttiṉuḷ avi tām 23peyar ai eṉṉum cūttirattuḷ 24peyari oḻinta ēḻum eṉak koḷka. 
ivaṟṟi 25ōttaṭivu paṟṟi muṉ kūṟātatu, viṉikkaṇ varuvaṉa perumpāṉmiyākal paṟṟip pōlum.
maṟṟi, aciniliyum, iciniṟiyum, kuṟippiṟ poruḷ 26ceykunavum ivvōttiṉuḷ tattam ciṟappuc cūttirattuḷḷē utāraṇam kāṭṭappaṭum.
oppil vaḻiyāṟ poruḷ 27ceykunavaṟṟiṟku utāraṇam itaṉ 28piṉṉatikārattuḷ uvamiyiyaluḷ kāṇappaṭum.
iccūttirattu muṉṉiya mūṉṟum muṉṉar ōtip pōntaṉa ākalāṉ muṉ vikkappaṭṭaṉa. piṉṉiyatu piṉṉati kārattuk kūṟalāl piṉ vikkappaṭṭatu. iṭiyaṉa ivvōt tiṉuḷ kūṟappaṭutalāṉ iṭiniṉṟaṉa. āyiṉ iṭikkaṇ niṉṟa mūṉṟaṉuḷḷum poruḷ ceyvataṉi muṉ 29collātu, acinili iciniṟikaḷi muṉ colliyatu eṉṉi eṉiṉ, ivi iraṇṭum oḻiya īṟṟiṉ muṉṉum piṉṉum niṉṟavi ellām 30perumpāṉmiyiṉaviyāy niṟṟalāṉum, ivitām 31aviyāy niṟṟalāṉum, ivi tām 32aviyāy niṟṟal ciṟupāṉmi yākalāṉum eṉpatu. 247.	avitām,
	muṉṉum piṉṉum moḻiaṭuttu varutalum
	tammīṟu tiritalum piṟitava ṇiliyalum
	aṉṉavi ellām uriya eṉpa.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, iṉṉum avaṟṟiṟku āvatōr viti uṇarttutal nutaliṟṟu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- mēl 1vakukkappaṭṭavi tām, muṉṉiṭattum piṉṉiṭattum peyarviṉiyākiya moḻikaḷi aṭintu varutalum, accoṟkaḷ tamīṟu oruvaḻi eḻuttu vēṟupaṭṭu varutalum, maṟṟōr iṭiccol tāṉ niṟkumiṭattē niṟṟalum ākiya attaṉmiyiyuṭiya ilakkaṇam 2ellāvaṟṟiṟkum ilakkaṇamātaṟku uriya eṉṟavāṟu.
(u-m.) 
muṉṉaṭuttatu: atumaṉ, 3kēṇmiyā eṉpaṉa. 
piṉṉaṭuttatu: koṉṉūr, 4ō otantār eṉpaṉa.
īṟu tirintatu: maṉṉik kāñci, iḵtottaṉa5eṉpaṉa. 
maṉṉic col, tilliccol eṉpaṉavō eṉiṉ, avi poruḷ uṇarttātu coltammi uṇara niṉṟa; āka lāṉ īṇṭikku ākā eṉpatu. maṟṟu 6eṉṉi tiripu peṟumāṟu eṉiṉ, uṭampoṭu puṇarttal eṉpataṉāṉ avaṟṟi ivvāṟu ōtiya cūttiraṅkaḷāl peṟutum7 eṉpatu.
piṟitavaṇiliyal: makaviṉi, maṭavi maṉṟamma eṉpatu.		(3)

251.	eccam ciṟappē aiyam etirmaṟi
	muṟṟē eṇṇē terinili ākkameṉ
	ṟappāl eṭṭē ummic collē.
eṉpatu eṉnutaliṟṟō eṉiṉ, ituvum atu.
itaṉ poruḷ:- eccattiṉ kaṇṇatum, ciṟappiṉ kaṇṇatum, aiyattiṉ kaṇṇatum, 1etirmaṟik kaṇṇatum, muṟṟiṉ 2kaṇṇatum, eṇṇiṉ kaṇṇatum, terinilik kaṇṇatum, ākkattiṉ kaṇṇatum eṉa 3ikkūṟu eṭṭām um eṉṉum col eṉṟavāṟu.
eccam iṟantatu4 taḻīiyatum, etiratu taḻīiyatum eṉa 5iru vakittu. 
muṉ cāttaṉ vantāṉ; piṉ koṟṟaṉum vantāṉ eṉpatu iṟantatu taḻīiyiṟṟu.
vēṟu oruvaṉum varum eṉpatu paṭa niṉṟu cāttaṉum varum eṉpatu etiratu taḻīiyiṟṟu.
iṉṉum ivveccam 6muḻuvatum orupuṭi taḻuvuvatum eṉa iruvakittu.
aviyāvaṉa:- yāṉ karuvūrkkuc celvēṉ eṉṟāṟku yāṉum avvūrkkup pōtuval eṉpatum, avvāṟu kūṟiṉārkku yāṉum uṟiyūrkkup 7pōtuvaṉ eṉpatūum eṉa ivi.
iṉic ciṟappu - uyarvu ciṟappum, iḻivu ciṟappum eṉa iru vakittu.
uyarvu - 8tēvarvēṇṭiṉum vēmpukikkum eṉpatu.
 ūrkku maṇittē poyki eṉpatum atu.
iḻipu - avvūrp pūciyum pulāṟṟiṉṉātu eṉa varum.
aiyam - pattum eṭṭum uḷa eṉpatu.
9ceytatu maṉṟu eṉpatum atu.
etirmaṟi - koṟṟaṉ varutaṟkum uriyaṉ eṉpatu. ivvetir maṟi aḵṟiṇi viravup peyariyalpu māṟuḷavē 10 eṉṟāṟpōlap paṇpu paṟṟiyum varum.
eccattōṭu itaṉiṭi vēṟṟumi eṉṉi eṉiṉ, atu piṟitōr poruḷiṉit taḻuvum, iḵtu apporuṭ ṭāṉum oru kūṟṟit taḻuvum eṉpatu.
iṉi muṟṟu:- tamiḻnāṭṭu mūvēntarum vantār eṉpatu. yātum ūrē 11nāḷumaṉṉāṉ pukaḻumaṉṉi eṉpaṉavum avi.
iṉi eṇ - nilaṉum nīrum eṉpatu. 
iṉit terinili - 12naṉṟu tītum aṉṟu eṉpatu 13iṭi nikarttatu eṉṟu ōr poruṇmiyit terivittu 14niṟṟalāṉ terinili eṉṟu āyiṟṟu.
iṉi ākkam:- neṭiyaṉum valiyaṉum āyiṉāṉ eṉpatu. iḵtu eṇṇaṉṟō eṉiṉ, oruporuḷ taṉṉiyē 15collu taliṉ aṉṟu āyiṟṟup pōlum.
maṟṟum itaṉ akattu vikaṟpamellām aṟintukoḷka.		(7)

kallāṭam piṉṉiṇippukaḷ
The recommendations of the Expert Committee which met at Madras and Thanjavur, examined and selected the manuscripts for publication, were readily accepted by the Government who decided to call these publications “The Madras Government Oriental Series” and appointed the Curator as the General Editor of thereof. Since then, a number of valuable works in various languages and dealing with various subjects has been edited by eminent scholars rioted for their expertness and erudition in the respective spheres of knowledge to which the works belong.
The following manuscripts have been published so far under the “Madras Government Oriental Series”.
Tamil.
1.	Kappal Sattiram.
2.	Anubhava Vaidya Muri – Volumes I to IV.
3.	Asthana Kolahalam.
4.	Upadesa Kandam – Volumes I to III.
5.	Colan Purvapattayam.
6.	Kongadesa Rajakkal.
7.	Sivajnanadipam.
8.	Datea Nayanar Vaiddiya Attavani.
9.	Vaiddiyakkalanciyam.
10.	Saptarishi Nadi – Volumes I to VI.
1.	Mesa
2.	Virusabha
3.	Mithuna
4.	Kataka
5.	Simha and
6.	Kanya Lagnams.
11.	Karnatakarajakkal Savistara Carittiram.
12.	Bharata Siddhantam.
13.	Pilli-p-pini Vakatam (on infamtile diseases) Volumes I and II.
14.	Mattuvakatam.
15.	South Indian Temple Inscriptions:
Volume I
Volume II to III – Parts I and II.
16.	Perur-k-kovi.
17.	Siddhanta Vilakkam.
18.	Anandarangan Kovi.
19.	Magha Purana Ammani.
20.	Kolacala Stalapuranam.

Coming to the present publication Kallatar’s Commentary on Tolkappiyam Collatikaram under the Madras Government Oriental Series, selected by the Expert Committee and sanctioned by the Government on G.O. (Ms.) No. 1947 – Education dated 26th November 1957, its edition was entrusted in the same year to Prof. T.P.Meenakshisundaranar, M.A., M.O.L., B.L., who was then the Chief Professor of Tamil, Presidency College, Madras. Due to a variety of cuses which need not to be detiled here, the work could not be brought out earlier than now. That it has taken such a long time to see the light of the day, does, in no way, detract from its merit as an important contribution to the Science of Tamil Grammar.
Of the various languages, the two most ancient and developed ones are Sanskrit and Tamil and the glory of the latter has not a little been enhanced by the monumental work ‘Tolkappiam’ which is more than the earliest extent work on Tamil Grammar, nay, in Tamil itself, since it portrays the colorful and purposeful social life of the Tamils, some hundreds of years ago. Whether the work has been named after the author or vice versa is an interesting point for researcher for going into. Some scholars are of the opinion that Tolkappia might have been so called becuse hestudied all ancient works (tolkappiankal) before he wrote his magnificent work on Grammar. Some others believe that he belonged to an ancient Brahmin-family called ‘Kappiyakkuti’ in Tamil and since he was a distinguished scion of that family, he came to be respectfully called ‘Tolkappiyar’. That he was the first of the twelve illustrious disciples of Akattiyamuni, styled as the Father of Tamil and his ‘Tolkappiyam’ was but based on his preceptor’s famous work of Grammar, called ‘Perakattiyam’ which has now been unfortunately lost to us, are also points that have not gined the acceptance of a majority of scholars. There is, however, no doubt about the fact that Tolkappiyar must have studied Sanskrit very well and been conversant with the Vedas, the Pratisakhyas, Nirukts etc.,
Researchers on Tolkappiyar’s date have not also had the last word sid on it. Peraciriyar says that he was the first student of Akattiyar and this does not help us in any way becuse the age of that sage has not been finally arrived at. Naccinarkkiniyar, the greatest of commentators, avers that Tolkappiyar wrote his magnum opus towards the end of the first deluge which has not also been determined finally. Nakkirar, the Cankam-celebrity of undunted courage and conviction, remarks thus “ïÅ  ïil¢r§f¤â‰F« eil¢ r§f¤â‰F«”. The late Marimali Atikal has placed him before Panini, the greatest of grammatical geniuses ever known to any language. Internal evidences cannot also be relied upon completely becuse this work, as the learned Editor is inclined to believe, has not come down to us in its original form. The Professor has quite pertinently aid in his enlightened Introduction thus: “The question may also be rised whether ‘Tolkappiyam’ is the work of one author or is one like ‘Manusmriti’ etc., the work of a family or school of authors.” He also observes that it can be assumed on the basis of the major portions of the work that the Satras of Tolkappiyam belong to the pre-Cankam period. The consensus of scholarly opinion is that Tolkappiyar should be assigned to the 4th Century B.C. it is needless to say that the date of the author is yet another subject where research-scholars have a vast scope for bringing their critical acumen into play and arriving at the truth, according to their lights.
‘Tolkappiyam’ which is sid to have had its imprimatur in the learned assembly of Pandian Makirti, under the distinguished presidentship of Atankottacan, and which runs to about 1,600 Sutras, are divided into three Atikaram’s or Sections viz., Eluttatikaram, Collatikaram ad Porulatikaram. While the first section deals with the Phonology of Tamil and the second section reats of Syntax and Morpholgy, the third one comprises the two divisions known as Akam and Puram, which together constitute all the things of the world. The science of Poetics may also be sid to be the theme of this section.
While Paripatal, Akananuru, Kalittoki etc., cannot be understood without ‘Tolkappiyam’, the latter, it is no exaggeration to say, cannot be understood without the excellent expositions of versatile commentators like Ilampuranar. The illustrious line of commentators on Tolkappiyam is certinly impressive. It is headed by Ilampuranar, also called ‘Uriyaciriyar’ by Cenavariyar, with the great respect, has alone covered all the three Sections of Tolkappiyam. He must have studied Tamil in the traditional way, sitting at the feet of great masters of Tamil learning and must have had many a commentary before him which has been irretrievably lost to us. The learned Editor rightly observes that this commentator is minly responsible for the preservation of Tolakappiyam. Cenavariyar who is next in the tradition has written an excellent commentary on Collatikaram. He seems to have been very well acquainted with Sanskrit Sciences. Peraciriyar, coming next to him, has commented only upon portions of Porulatikaram, Naccinarkkiniyar, the profile commentator of admirable erudition, seems to have written a commentary on all the three Sections, though that on the last three chapters of Porulatikaram has not yet come to light. The commentary of Teyvacciliyar who is chronologically later than Cenavariyar and Naccinarkkiniyar on Collatikaram is as lucid as it is instructive.
Then comes Kallatar whose commentary constitutes the present edition. His name and date have been very ably discussed by the Professor who has afforded much food for thought unto research – scholars. He is of the opinion that the present commentary may not represent the work of one particular individual known as Kallatar but may be the culmination of the efforts of a variety of commentators belonging to the tradition of Kallatar or “Kallata-acarya-parampari.” A close study of the commentary reveals that the author must have studied the three commentaries of Ilampuranar, Cenavariyar and Naccinarkkiniyar with critical eyes and adopted only the best, in the various portions of his commentary.
The present edition is based on nine manuscripts, portions of Kallatar’s commentary printed in the issues of Nanacampantam and a few printed forms prepared by the late C.R.Namaccivaya Mutaliyar. Of the nine manuscripts, two manuscripts belong to our Library, one being D. 55 which is the original paper-manuscript and the other being R. 967 which is buta copy made from D. 55, with a portion of the commentary corrected on the basis of printed editions. A paper-manuscript which came into this Library, from Bavanantam Pilli’s Collections has also been used by the Editor. This Library also obtined a manuscript of this work from the Maturi-t-tamil-e-Cankam Library. All these have been listed by the Editor who had gone about collecting various manuscripts before he settled down to edit the work assigned to him. It is true more manuscripts must be in existence but are not unfortunately avilable. May I join the Editor in his appeal to such of those persons as may have manuscripts of this work in their possession to send them on to us, so that they may be used in the subsequent editions of this work? Need it be sid that the help of such kindly people will be gratefully acknowledged by us?
In conclusion, I feel very much gratified to say that an excellent edition of Kallatar’s commentary of Tolkappiyam is now being placed before Tamil scholars, who, I am confident, will extend a warm welcome to it. It is my bounden duty to thank the Government of Madras which is always keen on promoting the cuse of advanced knowledge which is the peerless glory of India, for having sanctioned the present publication at considerable cost. I must also express my profound gratitude to Professor T.P.Meenakshisundaranar, for having left no stone unturned in bringing out this valuable edition. I am also beholden to the Jamalia Press, Madras, (now defunct) and the Director of Stationery and Printing, Madras, for having finished the work assigned to them with expedition and efficiency.
R.K.PARTHASARATHI,
Formerly Curator and General Editor,
Madras Government Oriental Services
Government Oriental Manuscripts Library,
Madras-5

INTRODUCTION
I.
Tolkappiyam is the most ancient grammatical work in Tamil now avilable. There are some who will bring the age of Tolkappiyam down to the fifth century A.D. it is very difficult to date that work so definitely. But one can assign to it a chronology relative to the so called Cankam poetry. The fifth century is assigned to it by a few scholars on the ground they Tolkappiyam reveals post-Cankam usages and developments. But all those who have had intimate knowledge of the development of Tamil Language from Cankam age onwards, viz., the great commentators and the renowned grammarians of the modern age are confident that Tolkappiyar describes a pre-Cankam Tamil structure, such as the peculiar usage of [1] viyankol, [2] the particles of comparison, [3] the behavior of the roots Cel, Vam kotu, la, etc., and [4] the mentioning of names like Pen and such others, and [5] the rules with reference to most of the common words of every-day occurrences. These conflicting theories can be reconciled by taking the later-day feature found in Tolkappiyam as later additions. Scholars of the present day are more or less agreed on the last few cutras in Marapiyal being interpolations, becuse at least some of them are unnecessary repetitions.
One may, therefore, conclude that we have not received Tolkappiyam in the shape in which it was left by the author. Even Ilampuranar, the so-called earliest commentator, complins that certin cutras are missing (Tol-Porul-Cut Tiram 45). A detiled study will reveal many more losses and gins. The present writer has elsewhere pointed out the basic difference of approach between a majority of cutras in Tolkappiyam Eluttatikaram and a few particular cutras in the same work elsewhere.
The differences may be due to the rules belonging to different ages such as for instance, the rules relating to the viyankol and the rule relating to the use of the suffix kal. There may be interpolations by later-day copyists and students of Tolkappiyam making marginal notes for filling up lacune, which they felt in the text, whenever they came to read or copy the book, centuries after the original was written.
If his is not accepted, the question may also be rised as to whether Tolkappiyam is the work of one author or is one like Manusmrti, etc., the work of a family or school of authors.
Eluthtikaram takes one view and Collatikaram another. But there are also differences even with reference to the theories relating to the same problemet for instance the basic form for the morpho-phonemic alternation of nivir and num. In spite of Cenavariyar’s attempted reconciliation, the difference remins much more fundamental than what he explins. These remarks are minly intended as nothing more than suggestions for future research.
For the present, it is assumed on the basis of the major portion of this work that the cutras of Tolkappiyam belong to the pre-cankam period. The other points rised by scholars assigning a post-cankam period have been deal with in Camana-tamil Ilakkiya Varalaru by the present writer. After Tolkappiyam, perhaps another school opposing this grammar, probably, that of Avinayam grew, thanks to the Sanskrit influence; and finally succeeded more or less in driving Tolkappiyar into oblivion. There was somewhere at the end of the first millennium A.D. an attempt to revive the school of Tolkappiyar. Tolkappiyam is often mentioned even in cuttirams quoted with reverence in Yapparunkala virutti, though as far as prosody is concerned his views were not accepted evidently becuse Tamil versification has moved far away from his Age. Ilampuranar is hiled as the first commentator, probably becuse he was the earliest to write a commentary on Tolkappiyam. There must have been traces of older studies on Tolkappiyam as ia proved by his own remarks. Becuse of all these circumstances this commentator is not in a position to be definite in his statements in many places. He seems to be a great protagonist of the Tolkappiyam School even as Pallavatariyar, as learnt from Mayilinatar’s commentary on Nannul, was a great protagonist of the Avinayam School. Gradually Hindu writers like Naccinarkkiniyar made a Risi of Tolkappiyar, as greatr as Vedic Risis.
II.
This work consists of three major divisions of (1) eluttatikaram which deals generally with what may be now called phonology, (2) Collatikaram which deals with what may be now called morphology and syntax and (3) Porulatikaram which generally deals with the subject matter of poetry, verses and poetics including prosody and poetic conventions. Of these Collatikaram has been studied by a number of authors becuse it gives what we may now describe as the grammatical structure.
We have a series of commentators on Collatikaram after Ilampuranar. In their hands every structure of Tamil becomes a Tolkappiyam usage. Though they feel that certin new changes have taken place even by the time of the Cankam Age, they try to bring them all within the four corners of Tolkappiyyam, though they are prepared to explin away later-day developments, as being due to the passage of time, a fact recognized by Tolkappiyar himself.  These commentaries are therefore valuable; for, without them for one thing, it will not be possible for us to understand the words and phrases used in Tolkappiyam. And for another, in the guise of interpretation, they write a new grammar of the Tamil of their own age. By careful study, one can distinguish in those commentaries, their own contributions and systematizations from the contributions of Tolkappiyar himself. In this way they form an important source for writing the History of the Tamil Language. The difference between Ilampuranar and Naccinarkkiniyar on Kurriyalukaram, for instance, occurring as utterance final and word final respectively, is really due to the respective ages in which they lived. After Ilampuranar came Cenavariyar; after him came peraciyar to be closely followed by Naccinarkkiniyar. Probably Kallatar came later as also Teyvacciliyar.
After these writers no one tried to write a commentary on Tolkappiyam. Instead, they introduced their researches on Tolkappiyam and is commentaries either in their own commentaries on Nannul, like Mayalinatar and Cankaranamaccivayar or in their own commentaries on their own works, like the authors of Ilakkana Vilakkam, Ilakkana k-kottu, Tonni and Muttuviriyam. Sivanana Swamikal in the guise of writing a commentary on the first cuttiram of Eluttatikaram has given us the results of his own researches. In the nineteenth century we have the editions of Tolkappiyam. Even in the ....th century scholars like Aracancannukanar, Aakkina-c-camikal, Marimali Atikal, P.S.Sastri, Mannarkuti Somasuntaram Pilli Venkata rajulu Reddiar and Venkatasami Nattar and others have only been translating writing commentaries, carrying on discussions or elucidating the problems of Tolkappiyam.
Today Tolkappiyam is in the hands of the students undergoing the Oriental and the Post-Graduate courses. Tolkappiyam Collatikaram with Cenacariyar’s commentary was published in the last century by Arumuka Navalar. Another edition was published by Srinivasa Raghava of the Presidency College. Ilampuranar’s commentary on Eludttu was published in this century by our patriot V.O. Chidambaram Pilli. His commentary on Porulatikaram was edited by Sri Vaiyapuri Pilli acting under the direction of V.O. Chidambaram Pilli, but unfortunately did not see the light of day. Sri Namaccivaya Mutaliyar brought out an edition of this work. Naccinarkkiniyar’s commentary on Collatikaram, as for those on other atikarams, was published by the late, lamented scholar and editor, Sri C.W.Damodaram Pilli in the nineteenth century itself. In this century Sri Venugopala Pilli published a new edition of Naccinarkkiniyar’s commentary on Collatikaram. Sri Ganesa Ayyar, the great scholar of Jaffna, unfortunately no more with us, has brought out a scholarly edition of Cenavariyar’s commentary with valuable foot notes. Kantaswamiyar, a scholar himself, trined under Aracanacanmukanar, brought out an edition published by South India Saiva Siddhantha Publishing Society, which has now issued a second edition with notes this time by Bhuvarakam Pilli. Karanti-t-tamil-c-Cankam which, under the leadership of Umamakeswaram Pilli, worked for Tamil culture and research, printed for the first time Teyvacciliyar’s commentary on Tolkappiyam. The commentary of Kallatar was printed in the pages of Nanacampantam by Sri Mahavidwan S.Dhandapani Desikar, but earlier to that attempt, Sri Namaccivaya Mutaliyar himself started printing Kallatdam in a book form but unfortunately it has not completed and only a few printed forms are avilable which were exhibited by the Saiva Siddhantha Publishing Society on the occasion of the release of their 1008th Publication.
III.
The advisory committee appointed by the Government of recommending Manuscripts to be printed by the Madras Government Oriental Manuscripts Library recommended the publication of Kallatar’s commentary on Tolkappiyam – Collatikaram. The present writer was requested to edit and a fair copy of the Manuscript was sent to him for being printed. He felt his function as an editor did not consist merely in sending to the press the Manuscript prepared by the copyists of the Library and certified to be correct. He therefore, went about collecting the Manuscripts. The Library itself got for him a copy of the Manuscript avilable in the Maturi-t-tamil-c-Cankam Library. Mr. S. Rajam of Murray and Company, arranged for the collection of this fair copy with the Cadjan leaf Manuscript contining the commentary of Kallatar, avilable in the Kalaksetra Library, Adyar, a manuscript belonging to Dr. Swaminatha Iyer. There was also another copy of the commentary which came into the Oriental Manuscripts Library from Bavanantam Pilli’s collections. Though Namaccivaya Mutaliyar’s edition was not avilable at that stage, Sri Venkatarajulu Reddiyar kindly gave his copy of that commentary. It was prepared from the Manuscripts on the basis of which Namaccivaya Mutaliyar’s edition was finalized. He also kindly gave the pages of the Magazine nanacampantam contining the Kallatar’s commentary. After the present edition was prepared and when the present writer came to serve in the Annamali University, another copy of Kallatar’s commentary became avilable in the Annamali University. The readings avilable in this last Manuscript could not be used in this edition. Therefore they are given later in this Introduction itself. There was also a copy of the commentary prepared by the present writer from the Manuscript copy avilable at the Manuscripts Library. The Manuscripts Library had an old Manuscript from which another copy was prepared and it is stated there in that a portion of the work had been corrected by comparison with the printed commentaries of Cenavariyar and Naccinarkkiniyar.
For convenience of reference these various manuscripts and printed works have been given Roman numbers in the foot notes where variant readings are noted. These are explined below.
[a] I is the original paper Manuscript in the Oriental Manuscripts Library. The paper used to look like newsprint and is already giving way crumbling to pieces in some places.
[b] II is a copy made from the above. But as already stated, the first portion has been corrected not on the basis of any other Manuscripts of Kallatar’s commentary but on the basis of printed editions of other commentaries. This manuscript is in a good condition of preservation. The hand-writing is much better than what is found in the first manuscript. It was this manuscript which was sent to the present writer duly copied and certified as correct, for being sent to the press. Simply becuse it was corrected by the copyist, it was not taken as the basis though the corrections in many places seem to be justified. But as faithfulness to the Manuscripts should be the first duty of the editor, the Present edition, has relied upon No. I. as the basic text. At the end of the commentary on sutram 254 this manuscript states that thereafter the rest is not avilable, though on the basis of No. I. it gives a few broken lines.
[c] III is the paper manuscript coming from the Bavanantam Academy. The second part of this volume is unfortunately not avilable and the first volume stops in the middle of the commentary on sutram 225. [With the end of the line 12 of pp. 300 of this edition; vide foot note 27 on that page.] The first volume is in a good condition of preservation and its handwriting is legible and good.
[d] IV is the Ms. Obtined from the Maturi-t-Tamil-c-cankam Library. It is a copy made from No. I and not from No. II. Therefore, this must have been prepared before No. II was prepared. But this is not a carefully-prepared copy. Many lines have been left out by the copyist. This is not helpful except in a few cases where portions in No. I have crumbled to pieces.
[e] V is the Ms. Copy prepared by Sri Venkatarajulu Reddiyar. It is written in the beautiful hand writing of Mr. Reddiyar himself. He has made his own corrections, but this corrections can be identified as may be seen from the foot notes to this edition. This stops with the end of the commentary on sutram 254. This agrees more with III.
[f] VI is the portion of Kallatar’s commentary printed in the issues of Nanacampantam by Sri. S.Dhandapani Desikar. This edition is prepared on the basis of No. II. But Sri. Desikar has made many alternations which are probably required for making any sense of the text in each places.
[g] VII is the Ms. Copied from No. II by the present writer, who had made his own corrections where ever he thought it necessary. This stops with sutram 254.
[h] VIII is the Ms. which belonged to Dr. Swaminata Iyer and which is now avilable at the KalakSetra Library. This has been responsible for restoring some of the portions not avilable in other copies. On the Ms. there is a statement that it is the copy of Satacivam Pilli if Jaffna, who was himself a disciple of Arumuka Navalar. This also stops with the commentary on sutram 254.
[i] IX is the Ms. copy avilable at the Annamali University Library. But this is only the second volume of the commentary. It begins with the sutram 107 and its commentary. It is written on good paper which resists decay. As already stated this was not avilable when the present edition was printed and therefore this is utilized in an Appendix hereto. This Manuscripts stops with words “ariccollina –p-pinvarum collatu” on line 1 if page 344 of the present edition.
[j] X is the printed edition prepared by the late scholar C.R.Nanaccivaya Mutaliyar. But only a few formes are avilable.

The manuscripts may be arranged in the form of genealogical treed given above. Under the circumstances explined above it was not possible to reconstruct the manuscripts and then to proceed with the edition. This has therefore to be done at a later date.
These are all the editions and manuscripts which came to the knowledge of the present writer. It is unfortunate that other manuscripts which must be in existence have not been avilable. The present writer will be grateful to those who can send in the manuscripts of this work in their possession so that they can be used at least in the second edition, with their help being gratefully acknowledged.
The editor has not altered a single letter except on the authority of any original text. Naturally the texts like II, VI and VII could not be treated as basic texts for they have admittedly altered the texts as found in No. I and No. II. In the result only the readings in I, III and V and VIII have been studied for preparing the text and whenever one of the readings therein was found preferable, the editor has adopted it. If such help was not forthcoming from these basic texts he has left the reading as it is without trying to change it. But the footnote mentions all the readings, even those from altered texts and sometimes he has pointed out that such readings might have been the original. He has followed this policy even with reference to the quotations found from classics, without taking the liberty of correcting them on the basis of printed texts avilable from them in the hope that these variant readings even when not clear and sometimes full of mistakes may help scholars to find out for themselves the correct readings for the text quoted. He has in the footnotes pointed out the ambiguities possible in the scripts which may help to explin the different readings. A book on Paleography may make use of the explanations. It is unfortunate that it is not possible to give the Photostat copies of at least a few specimen leaves from these various manuscripts. The editor has not refrined from giving even what may appear to be absurd and impossible readings when they are so found. For one thing it is impossible to say that these may not be required at a later stage of research. We have only to note how readings rejected as absured being welcomed by recent researches on the texts of Shakespeare. For another, these mistakes even if they are such, throw some light on the pronunciation habits of the copyists and on the writing system of their day. This will be helpful to those who write the history of the colloquial Tamil language and the History of Tamil Writing System.
It is unfortunate that we know precious little about the commentator Kallatar. We have a poet of this name in the Cankam Age. There is another as the author of Kannappar Maram found included in the eleventh Saiva Tirumuri. There is also the author of the poetic work Kallatam, who probably, came a little later. The author of our commentary is probably the fourth person of the same name of literary eminence. Kallatam is referred to as the name of a place by Manikkavacakar in his Tiruvacakam. Perhaps, this name has therefore nothing to do with Kallata, found as the name of a great writer in Kashmi Saivim.
Kallatar’s commentary is referred to only by Subramanya Dhiksitar in his own commentary on his Tamil Grammatical Work, Pirayoka Vivekam. He belongs to the early years of the eighteenth century. That fixes only the lower limit for the age of this commentary Naccinarkkiniyar who refers by name to Cenavariyar and Ilampuranar deos not mention Kallatar. But it must be sid he does not mention the name of every one whose theory he criticizes or refers to. But the commentary, as it stands today, is full of passages from Ilampuranar, Cenavariyar and Naccinarkkiniyar and in many places the language of the commentary cannot be on any grounds be assigned to any age older than Naccinarkkiniyar’s.
There is however, one important statement to be made. As one knows very well from the commentary on Iriyanar Akapporul, a commentary can be handed over as a tradition by word of mouth from teacher to student for many generations. In such a case one cannot say that the particular words in which the interpretation is written down at a later date where the very words used by the earliest authors. Here one can be sure of only the general idea; but even there, there is no guarantee that they are really ancient; for, the tradition naturally makes subsequent improvements elaborations and alternations even with reference to these general ideas. In such a case one is justified in taking the written commentary when avilable as the culmination of the tradition of a school rather than of any single author. In this sense, it may be possible to assign this commentary to one such tradition, specially characterized by the thoughts referred to as Kallatar’s in Pirayoka Vivekam, whereas in other cases the teachers of that tradition must have accepted the ideas of other commentators according to their own likes, and dislikes, sometimes accepting the view of one commentator and at other times, the view of other commentators, who all necessarily must have become famous by the time of this commentary came to be reduced writing in the sixteenth or seventeenth Century after Naccinarkkiniyar. Naccinarkkiniyar thus will fix the upper limit for this written form of commentary, though not for the tradition of this commentary itself.
The first sutram has an elaborate commentary following probably the style of the commentary on Iriyanar Akapporul. How far this goes back to the original tradition one cannot now assert. The various copies of the commentary itself seem to show that much emphasis was not placed on copying or preserving the exact words. One has only to refer to the beginning portions of every one of these sutras which goon varying.

cū. 1
vaḻuvamiyāc colliṟkē ilakkaṇam kūṟavēṇṭum.ēḻu vaḻuvakuttuk kūṟiṉāl vaḻu vilakkaṇam evvāṟu vaḻuvamiyāc colliṟku ilakkaṇamātal kūṭum? vaḻuppōla varutalākātu eṉa iṉmi mukattāl allatu etir maṟimukattāl kūṟiyatāka ivvaḻu vārāycciyik koḷḷutal vēṇṭum eṉpatu inta urippakutiyiṉ poruḷākum
eṉṉutal eṉṟu kūṟuka.
cū. 1
eḻuttōṭu puṇarntu poruḷaṟivikkum ōci ilakkaṇattil ārāyappaṭuvatutāṉ iyalpu. eḻuttōṭu puṇarntē (puṇarntiruntum) poruḷi valiyuṟuttātu varuvaṉavaṟṟiyum ivvatikāram ārāyvatākak kūṟuvatu aiyatti viḷivikkiṉṟatu. ivaṟṟi vaḻuveṉak kāṭṭi ārāykiṉṟatu pōlum.
cū. 1
ivvōttu eṉṉa peyarttō eṉiṉ - itaṉi “ivvōttu eṉṉa peyarttō?” eṉṉa - eṉpatākak koḷka. inta uriyil ivvāṟu kēḷvik kuṟiyōṭu muṭiyum coṟṟoṭarkaḷi “	” ikkuṟiyīṭukaḷiṉ iṭiyē varuvaṉa vākak koḷka. 6-17.
cū. 1
piṟa ottukkaḷil (iyalkaḷil) coṟkaḷ poruḷkaḷ mēlāyum niṉṟapōtu avaṟṟiṉ ilakkaṇaṅkaḷi uṇarttiṉār. āṉāl coṟkaḷ poruḷkaḷ mēlāmāṟṟiyē inta iyal viḷakkukiṟatu.
8-ep. 1.65.111. vakiya lallatilli eṉṟirukkumāṉāl iraṇṭu lakarattiṟkumēl mūṉṟu lakaram eṇṇikkiyil tavaṟi eḻutiviṭṭatākak koṇṭāl ippatippil koṇṭa pāṭamum ataṉāl ottuppōkum. itaṉiyē aṭikkuṟippu viḷakkukiṟatu.
cū. 1
eṉṟār eṉṟaṟpālataṉi - iṅku ṉakarattiṉ mēṟpuḷḷi paḻiya ēṭukaḷil iruppataṟkilli. eṉavē ataṉi uyir meyyākak koḷḷalām. aṭuttu ṟakarattiṟkup patilāka iraṇṭiṉit tavaṟāka eḻutiyatākak koḷḷalām. appōtu “eṉṟār eṉaṟpālataṉi” āṉāl kiṭitta pāṭaṅkaḷi lellām acciṭṭapaṭiyēyuḷḷamiyāl avvāṟē patippikka vēṇṭiyatāyiṟṟu.
cū. 1
oru cūttirattil pala coṟṟoṭarkaḷ varalām. appōtu poruṭpaṭi allatu poruṭpaṭuttuvatu eṉṟu kūṟakkūṭiya viṉiccol coṟṟoṭaril īṟṟil varum. avvāṟu oru vākkiyam muṭintapiṉ maṟṟōr vākkiyam varumāṉāl mutal vākkiyattiṉ īṟṟuc colliṟkum, iraṇṭām vākkiyattiṉ mutaṟcolliṟkum poruḷ toṭarpō aḵtāvatu poruḷiyipō allatu colliyipō iruppataṟkilli. ikkālattil itaṉi arippuḷḷi iṭṭu viḷakkuvōm; muḻup puḷḷiyiṭṭāl cūttiram oṉṟāmāṟilli.
cū. 4
ivveṉa-iv eṉpataṟku iviyeṉṟē poruḷ. 13-ep. eṉa 1.98 kūṟiyatupōl iṅkuk koḷḷalākātu.
cū. 7
ivvari iṅku utāraṇamāmāṟu illi. “ārttār koṇṭatu vantār” eṉpatōṭu cērntuvanta ceyyuṭpakuti ākalāṉ. 
cū. 11
kīḻkkuṟippil koṇṭa pāṭam poruntalām.
cū. 15
muṭaṅkuttiṟṟu - muṭaṅkiṟṟu
cū. 16
uṇṭēṉum eṉpatu uṇṭēlum eṉa māṟiyatu uriyip paṭiceyvār kālattatā uriyeḻutiya kālattatā eṉṟu ārāytal vēṇṭum.
cū. 17
cōḻanāṭu, arumaruntaṉṉāṉ mutaliya pōla ilakkaṇa “vāypāṭu aminta vaḻakkukaḷum uḷvaḻi arumaruntāṉa, cōṇāṭu eṉac coṟ citiyavum varuvaṉa ilakkaṇattoṭu poruntā marūu vaḻakku allatu marūu vaḻakku eṉappaṭum.
cū. 25
piṉṉum viṉiyiyaluḷ aṉmi iṉmi eṉpavaṟṟip paṇpup peyarākavē koḷvar. 263-ām pārkka.
cū. 32
ainterutu uṭiyavaṉoruvaṉ taṉ erutukaḷi avaṟṟik kāppavaṉiṭattil koṭuppa, piṉṉar avaṟṟil ōrerutu kāṇāmaṟ pōykkeṭṭatu. ataṉik kūṟavanta kāppāṉ “oṉṟu keṭṭatu” eṉṟu uṭiyavaṉiṭattuc colliṉāṉ. avvāṟu colliṉati yaṭuttu erutiṉiuṭiyāṉ “veḷḷiyerutō kārerutō keṭṭatu” eṉṉum aiyantīrtaṟporuṭṭu “avaṟṟuḷ yātu” eṉṟu viṉāvutalē iṅkuk kuṟikkappaṭuvatu. naṭu eṉpatu 5-āvatu variyil naṭu eṉa uḷḷatu viḷaṅkavilli; iṭiyeṉṟu itaṉit tirittum payaṉilli. colluvatu uṭiyāṉiṭiyēyaṉṟik kāppāṉiṭiyēyaṉṟu. ellāp piratikaḷilum iṅkē eḻuttukkaḷ iruttalāl 5-ām piratiyiṉ pāṭattik koḷvataṟkilli. “kēṭu” eṉṟu irukkalām.
cū. 41
koḷḷappaṭum eṉpataṉik koḷḷutal vēṇṭum eṉṟu poruḷ koḷka. illiyāṉāl avaṟṟi eṉpatu tavaṟum. itu tirukkuṟaḷilum varum vaḻakku.
cū. 58
eṉṉieṉa varum iraṇṭiloṉṟu miki pōlum.
cū. 58
intac cūttirattil varum kālam mutaliyaṉavum, muṉ cūttirattil varum kuṭimi mutaliyaṉavum uyartiṇip poruḷil vantum aḵṟiṇi muṭipu koḷḷukiṉṟaṉavātaliṉ iraṇṭiyumoṉṟākki oru cūttiramāka ōtātatu eṉṉi eṉak kēḷvi eḻukiṟatu. kuṭimi mutaliyaṉavellām paṇpaṭiyāṉ niṉṟu paṇpuṭiya poruḷik kuṟittu apporuḷ mēl nikaḻkiṉṟaṉa (varukiṉṟaṉa). ātaliṉ avaṟṟi muṉṉattiṉuṇaruṅ kiḷavieṉa oruvakiyākki avvāṟu muṉṉattiṉuṇarap paṭuvaṉavākak kiḷavātu varum kālum mutaliya peyarkaḷi vēṟōr vakiyākki āciriyar kūṟiṉār eṉpatu akkēḷvikku viṭi. oṉpatāvatu variyil oruvayiyāṟa kiḷavātu varum eṉṟa pakuti viḷaṅkavilli. “oruvakiyākkik kiḷavātu varum” eṉṟu pāṭam koḷḷavēṇṭum pōlum.
cū. 63
poruḷnili eṉpatu poruḷ nilimi eṉṟirukkalām.
cū. 71
kallāṭar viḷakkam pārāṭṭaṟkuriyatu
cū. 71
 aṉṟittāṉē eṉpatil tāṉē acipōlum.
cū. 72
itu viṉikkuṟippu eṉpatu aṅku vēṇṭāta varipōlum iṅkē 10-ām vari “itu viṉikkuṟippu” eṉa varuvatu tāyik kātalaṉ eṉpatōṭu maṭṭum cellum pōlum. illiyāṉāl ceyyum eṉṉum muṟṟu kuṟippu viṉiyākavum varueṉak koṇṭār eṉṟu kūṟa vēṇṭi varum.
cū. 76
mutal eṉpataṟku ittōṭṭam eṉṟa poruḷi uyttuṇar eṉkiṟār uriyāciriyar eṉalām.
cū. 77
 “	” ticikku eṉpatu urupākātē, varum urupiṟku kaṇ mutalāṉa. “vaṭakkaṇ vēṅkaṭam mutaliya iṭaṅkaḷil” ticikkūṟu eṉṟa poruḷik kuṟikkum eṉpatic cuṭṭa; “tēvaki . . . ticikkūṟu itu poruḷ” eṉṟu eḻutukiṟār.
cū. 77
“ōriṭaṅkaḷiyum” eṉpatu “ōriṭattiyum” eṉṟiruntāl poruḷ viḷaṅkum. “allaṉavō iṭaṅkaḷiyum variyaṟuttu” eṉṟumirukkalām.
cū. 101
“tokka nillā” itu tokku nillā eṉṟiruttal vēṇṭum.
cū. 110
ākupeyariyum aṉmoḻittokiyiyum ōciyāl vēṟupaṭuttu kiṉṟār vākkiyattil vārātapōtu pōlum.

cū. 187

cū. 210

 vaṉmi, valimi eṉa uruvam vēṟupaṭṭamiyāl poruḷum nāḷaṭivil vēṟupaṭṭatu.
cū. 227
“ciṉiviṉi mutalviṉiyoṭu” eṉpatupaṟṟiyē iṅku eḻuntatu ārāycci. avvāṟāṉāl ōr aiyam eḻukiṟatu. “mutala (cāttaṉ) eḻuvāy vēṟṟumiyāka vārāmal vēṟu vakiyāka varuvatum uṇṭu. cāttaṉatu kiyiṟṟu vīḻntatu eṉa varum viṉimuṭipu ic cūttirattiṉ vitikoṇṭu “cāttaṉatu kiyiṟṟu vīḻntāṉ” eṉak koḷḷavum kūṭumaṉṟō” eṉpatē iṅkeḻum kēḷvi. avvāṟu varutalākātu eṉpatu umirayāciriyar kūṟum muṭipu. ciṉi mutaloṭu muṭital. ic cūttirattiṉ vitikoṇṭu “cāttaṉatu kiyiṟṟu vīḻntāṉ” eṉak koḷḷavum kūṭumaṉṟō eṉpatē iṅkeḻum kēḷvi. avvāṟu varutalākātu eṉpatu uriyāciriyar kūṟum muṭivuṣapu. ciṉi mutaloṭu muṭital iccūttirattil kūṟappeṟṟuḷḷatu; atu aṅṅaṉam nikaḻvatu mutal eḻuvāyākip payaṉiliyām muṭivukoḷḷum vaḻiyē eṉa ivvuriyāciriyar viḷakkukiṟār.

VIII
It is with great pleasure that I acknowledge my indebtedness to the Advisoryt Committee who entrusted me with this work, to the successive Curators who rendered all the help necessary, to Sri S. Rajam who made it possible for meto collate tghe present edition with the valuable manuscript of Dr. Swaminata Aiyar, to the authorities of Dr. Swaminata Aiyar’s Library for allowing this study of collation through my assistant, Sri Venkatarajalu Reddiar who so kindly lent me his manuscript copy and his copy of Nanacampantam, to Sri K.Arumugam, M.A., M.Litt., Reader in Tamil, Delhi University, who pointed out the mistakes in the printed text, to Sri P. Arunachalam, Sri E. Annamali, M.A., Lectyurers, Tamil (Arts) Department and Sri G. Vijayavenugopal, M.A., for preparing the concordance of Collatikaram commentaries, to Kumari A. Parameswari and Kumari N. Rajalakshmi, the University Grants Commission Research Scholars for preparing the readings avilable from the Annamali University Manuscript, and lastly to the authorities of Annamali University for permitting to use their valuable manuscript. Nothing in this world is an unmixed happiness. I am very sorry that my good friend Sri Sivaprakasam Pilli, the copyist of the Government of Madras Oriental Manuscript Library who so willingly co-operated with me in collating all the manuscripts and copies, in preparing the copy for print and in correcting, proofs, is not now alive to see this book in its final form. Sri Venkatarajulu Reddiar also in no more with us. I owe deep debt of gratitude to them and I only hope their souls will rest in peace and bless this edition now that the book has been placed before the scholarly public.
T. P. MEENAKSHISUNDARAM.



kallāṭaṉār virutti (vēṟu uri)
am: mūṉṟu kūṟṟuc collum uyartiṇiyi aṟivikkum collām.
avvē eṉa āṟāvataṉ paṉmiyākak koḷka.      (2)
3. 	oṉṟaṟi collē palavaṟi colleṉ
	ṟāyiru pāṟ co laḵṟiṇi yavvē.
e-tu āṇ peṇṇākiya oṉṟaṉiyum avviraṇṭiṉatu paṉmiyākiya palavaṟṟiyum aṟivikkum colloṭu poruntiya avviraṇṭu kūṟṟuc collum aḵṟiṇiyi aṟivikkum collām.
uyartiṇiyavvē, aḵṟiṇiyavvē e-tu, uyartiṇi eṉa am muppāṟ col aḵṟiṇi eṉa āyirupāṟcol eṉa.
ē īṟṟaci.
4.	peṇmi cuṭṭiya uyartiṇi maruṅki
	ṉāṇmi tirinta peyarnilik kiḷaviyun
	teyvañ cuṭṭiya peyarnilik kiḷaviyu
	mivveṉa vaṟiyu mantan tamakkilavē
	uyartiṇi maruṅkiṟ pālpiṟin ticikkum.
e - tu peṇmit taṉmi eytiya uyartiṇiyiṭattu āṇmi nīṅkiya peyarp poruḷum teyvañ cuṭṭiya peyarp poruḷum pālaṟiya niṟkum īṟṟeḻuttu it taṉmiyaṉa eṉat tamakku uṭiyavalla; uyartiṇi muppāliṉiyum viḷakkum eḻuttiṉiyē īṟāka icikkum.
‘u - m : - pēṭi vantāṉ, pēṭi vantāḷ, pēṭiyar vantār.
vācutēvaṉ vantāṉ, tiruviṉāḷ vantāḷ, muppattu mūvarum vantār eṉavarum. uyartiṇi maruṅkiṉ eṉpataṉi āṇmi cuṭṭiya eṉṟu moḻimāṟṟi urikka. antan tamakkilavē eṉṟataṉāṟ nākaṉ vantāṉ, nāki vantāḷ, nākar vantār eṉak tēvarum makkaḷum allāta nākarkkum am muṭivu koḷka.
5.	ṉaḵkā ṉoṟṟē yāṭūu vaṟicol.
e - tu, ṉakara voṟṟē āṇmakaṉi aṟivikkum col.
u - m : - uṇṭāṉ. uṇṇā niṉṟāṉ. uṇpāṉ eṉa mūṉṟu kāla viṉiyāṉum, kariyaṉ ceyyaṉ eṉak kālan tōṉṟāta viṉik kuṟippāṉum aṟika.
6.	ḷaḵkā ṉoṟṟē makaṭūu vaṟicol.
e - tu.
u - m : - uṇṭāḷ, uṇṇā niṉṟāḷ, uṇpāḷ eṉṉum mūṉṟu kāla viṉiyāṉum, kariyaḷ, ceyyuḷ eṉṉum viṉikkuṟippāṉum uṇarka.
7.	raḵkā ṉoṟṟum pakara viṟutiyum
	mārik kiḷavi yuṭpaṭa mūṉṟum
	nērat tōṉṟum palaraṟi collē.
u - m : - uṇṭār, uṇṇā niṉṟār, uṇpār eṉṉum mūṉṟu kāla viṉiyāṉum; kariyār, ceyyār eṉṉum viṉik kuṟippāṉum uṇpa, tiṉpa, koṇmār vantār eṉa etirkāla muṟṟuc collāṉum aṟika.
8.	oṉṟaṟi kiḷavi taṟaṭa vūrnta
	kuṟṟiya lukarat tiṟatiyākum
u - m : - uṇṭatu, uṇṇā niṉṟatu, uṇpatu; karitu, ceytu eṉak kālam mūṉṟāṉum, viṉik kuṟippāṉum, kūyiṟṟu; tāyiṟṟu; kōṭiṉṟu; kuḷampiṉṟu; ukiriṉṟu; mayiriṉṟu eṉa iṟanta kālattāṉum viṉik kuṟippāṉum, kuṇṭukaṭṭu; koṭuntāṭṭu eṉṟa viṉikkuṟippāṉum taṟaṭakkaḷi ūrntuvanta kuṟṟiyalukattāṉ oṉṟaṟicol eṉṟu aṟika.
9.	a ā va veṉa varūu miṟuti
	yappāṉ mūṉṟē palavaṟi collē.
u - m : - uṇṭaṉa; uṇṇā niṉṟaṉa; uṇpaṉa; kariya; ceyya eṉak kālaṅkaḷāṉum, viṉikkuṟippāṉum uṇṇā; tiṉṉā eṉṉum muṟṟuc collāṉum uṇkuva; tiṉkuva eṉa etirkālattāṉum āka a ā va eṉṉum am mūṉṟu collāṉum paṉmiyāṉum uṇarappaṭum eṉṟu aṟika.
10.	irutiṇi maruṅki ṉimpā laṟiya
	vīṟṟuniṉ ṟicikkum patiṉō reḻuttun
	tōṟṟan tāmē viṉiyoṭu varumē.
u - m : uyartiṇiyiṭattum aḵṟiṇiyiṭattum oruvaṉ, orutti, palar, oṉṟu, pala eṉṉum aintu pālum aṟiya iṟutiyiṉiṉṟuṇarttum patiṉōreḻuttum pulappaṭu miṭattu viṉiyoṭu naṭakkum. aviyāvaṉa : - ṉakāramum ḷakāramum rakāramum pakāramumākum. tavvum ṟavvum ṭavvum avvum āvum vavvum eṉa ivi muṉcoṉṉa cūttiraṅkaḷiṉ viṉiyoṭu naṭakkumāṟu kaṇṭukoḷka.
11.	viṉiyiṟ ṟōṉṟum pālaṟi kiḷaviyum
	peyariṟ ṟōṉṟum pālaṟi kiḷaviyu
	mayaṅkal kūṭāta tammara piṉavē.
e - tu. viṉiyiṉāṟ pālaṟiyappaṭum poruḷum, peyariṉāṟ pālaṟiyappaṭum poruḷum mayaṅkutalip poruntā. avi tattam ilakkaṇattaṉavāyē varum.
u - m : - uṇṭāṉ avaṉ, uṇṭāḷ avaḷ, uṇṭār avar, uṇṭatu atu, uṇṭaṉa avi eṉa viṉi niṟpa peyarmēl tattam marapiṉāṉ vantaṉa.
avaṉ uṇṭāṉ, avaḷ uṇṭāḷ, avar uṇṭār, atu uṇṭatu, avi uṇṭaṉa eṉa ivip peyar niṟpa viṉimēl tattam marapiṉāṉ vantaṉa.
iṉi mayaṅkal kūṭā eṉavē avaṟṟu mayakkam uṇṭeṉ patu collappaṭṭatām. am mayakkam eḻuvakippaṭum.
avi āvaṉa :- tiṇi mayaṅkivantuḻi vaḻuveṉṟu koḷka (kaka)
12.	āṇmi tirinta peyarnilik kiḷavi
	yāṇmi yaṟicoṟ kākiṭa ṉiṉṟē
emmūr aracaṉ nallaṉō ummūrk kōlikaṉ nallaṉō eṉṟum ciṉiyu mutalum piṟaḻap porūuvaṟka.
uppakka nōkki ṉupatēci tōṇmaṇantā
ṉuttara māmaturik kacceṉpa - vippakka
mātāṉa paṅki maṟuvil pukaḻc cennāp
pōtār puṉaṟ kūṭaṟ kaccu.
mātāṉapaṅkiyāvār tiruvaḷḷuva tēvar.
naṟunīla neytaluṅ koṭṭiyun tīṇṭip
piṟanāṭṭup peṇṭir muṭināṟum pāri
paṟanāṭṭup peṇṭi raṭi.
mēṟṟā ṉirunta vuyarciṉik koṉṟāṉiṟ
ṟēṟṟā voḻukkam paṭuvaṉakoṉ - māṟṟār
uṟumuraṇ cāyttā ṉuyarkuṭanti yāṭṭic
ciṟu maruṅkul ceṟṟa muli.
eṉṉum pāṭṭukkaḷ mutalum ciṉiyum piṟaḻntaṉa vāyiṉum piṟaḻvalla eṉṟu ettuṇiyaṉa porūuka.
in naṅki kaṇ nallavō, kayal nallavō eṉavum, pulipōlap pāyntāṉ eṉavum, maḻi vaṇki eṉavum, tuṭipōl naṭu eṉavum, vēypōlun tōḷ eṉavum, poṉpōlu mēṉi muttuppōlum pal eṉavum vantaṉavāl eṉiṉ aṉṉavi piṟaḻvalla. eṉṉi?
viṉipaya meyyuru veṉṟa nāṉkē
vakipeṟa vanta vuvamat tōṟṟam eṉpataṉāluṟaḻka.
apporuḷākum eṉṟataṉāl eṇṇumiṭattu muttum pavaḷamum maṇiyum poṉṉum eṉṟeṇṇuka. muttuṅ karuviḷam paralu maṇiyuṅ kāṉaṅ kōḻiyum poṉṉum eṉṟu eṇṇaṟka.	(kacu)
17.	takutiyum vaḻakkun taḻīiyiṉa voḻukum
	pakutik kiḷavi varinili yilavē.
e - tu : takuti paṟṟiyum vaḻakkup paṟṟiyum porunti naṭakkum ilakkaṇap pakutic col kaṭiyappaṭā.
takuti iraṇṭu vakippaṭum : - maṅkala marapiṉāṟ kūṟutalum iṭakkar aṭakkik kūṟutalum eṉa.
cettārit tuñciṉār eṉṟalum, cuṭukāṭṭi naṉkāṭu eṉṟalum, ōliyit tirumukam eṉṟalum, viḷakkup perukiṟṟu eṉṟalum ittoṭakkattaṉa maṅkala marapu.
kaṇkaḻīi varutum, kāṉmēl nīr peytuvarutum, antitoḻutuvarutum, kikkuṟiyarāyiruntār, poṟi yuyirttār eṉṉun toṭakkattaṉa iṭakkaraṭakku.
iṉi vaḻakkāvatu nāṉku vakippaṭum. avi, ilakkaṇa vaḻakku, ilakkaṇattōṭu poruntiya marūvuvaḻakku, marūvuvaḻakku, kuḻuviṉ - vanta kuṟunila vaḻakku eṉpavaṟṟuḷ nilam, nīr, tī, vaḷi, ākāyam, cōṟu, kūḻ, pāl, pāvitam, makkaḷ, maram, teṅku, kamuku ittoṭakkattaṉa ilakkaṇa vaḻakku.
iṉmuṉ eṉpataṉi muṉṟil eṉṟum, kaṇmī eṉpataṉi mīkaṇ eṉṟum, yāvar eṉpataṉi yār eṉṟum varutal ilakkaṇattoṭu poruntiya varūuvaḻakku.
arumaruntaṉṉāṉ eṉpataṉi arumaruntāṉ eṉṟum, potuvil eṉpataṉip potiyil eṉṟum, maliyamāṉāṭu eṉpataṉi malāṭu eṉṟum, cōḻaṉāṭu eṉpataṉic cōṇāṭu eṉṟum collutaṉ marūu vaḻakku.
poṟkollar poṉṉip paṟi eṉṟalum, vaṇṇakkar kāraṇatti nīlam eṉṟalum, yāṉippākar āṭiyik kūṟi eṉṟalum, iḻiciṉar cōṟṟic coṉṟi eṉṟalum kuḻuviṉ vanta kuṟunila vaḻakku.
iṉi oru cārār kuḻuviṉvanta kuṟunilavaḻakkum, iṭakkaraṭakkalum maṅkala marapiṉāṟ kūṟutalum eṉṟa im mūṉṟiyum takkavāṟu collutalāṉ takuti eṉṟākki, oḻintavaṟṟiyellām vaḻakkāṟu eṉpa.
iṉi, avar vaḻakkāṟu collumpaṭi ; karitu veḷitu eṉ kiyum, kiḻakku mēṟku eṉkiyum, ciṟitu periyatu eṉkiyum, karumi cemmiyākiya paṇpiṉālaṉṟi veḷḷāḷari veṇkaḷamar eṉṟum puliyarik karuṅkaḷamar eṉṟum cātipaṟṟi veḷḷāṭu eṉṟum collutal vaḻuvaṉṟu; cevvaḻakku eṉpa, taḻīiyaṉa oḻukum eṉṟamiyāl eṉpa.
18.	iṉaccuṭ ṭillāp paṇpukoḷ peyarkkoṭi
	vaḻakkā ṟalla ceyyu ḷāṟē.
e - tu. iṉattic cuṭṭātu paṇpu koṇṭu niṉṟa peyarc coṟkaḷ vaḻakku neṟiyalla, ceyyuḷ neṟiyām.
u - m: - ceññāyiṟṟuk kaviṉa, veṇkōṭṭi yāṉi, neṭuveṇ tiṅkaḷ, veṇmuttam eṉac ceyyuḷuḷ varum.
iṉik kuṟuñcūli, neṭuntakaṭi, kuṟumūkki, kuṟumpūḻ eṉpaṉavum iṉañcuṭṭātu vaḻakkiṉil vantaṉavāl eṉiṉ, avi avaṟṟiṉ pērē; iṉañcuṭṭatu vantaṉa alla eṉṟu maṟukka.
19.	iyaṟkip poruḷi yiṟṟeṉak kiḷattal.
e - tu. ulakattiyalpāki vārāniṉṟa poruḷkaḷic collumiṭattittaṉmi yeṉṟē colluka.
u - m : - nilam valitu, nīr taṇṇitu, tī veytu, vaḷi uḷarum, uyir uṇarum eṉa ivvāṟu colluvatu.
iṉi, nilam valitāyiṟṟu, nīr taṇṇitāyiṟṟu eṉṟu collaṟka. maṟṟu nilam valitāyiṟṟu eṉṟum collupavāl eṉiṉ, aḵtu iyalpaṉṟu. cēṟṟunilam mitittu vaṉṉilam mitikkiyāl vikāram eṉpatu.
20. 	ceyaṟkip poruḷi yākkamoṭu kūṟal.
e - tu. ceyaṟkiyākiya poruḷkaḷi ellām ākkaṅ koṭuttuc colluka.
u - m:- mayir nalla āyiṉa, piṅkūḻ nalla āyiṉa eṉac colluka. āyiṉa eṉpatu ākkam.
21.	ākkan tāṉē kāraṇa mutaṟṟē.
e - tu. av ākkantaṉṉic collumiṭattuk kāraṇa muṉ vittu ākkattip piṉ vittuc colluka.
u - m :- kaṭuvuṅ kipiḻi eṇṇeyum peṟṟamiyāṉ mayir nalla āyiṉa eṉavum, eruppeytu iḷaṅkaḷikaṭṭu nīrkālyāttamiyāṟpiṅkūḻ nalla āyiṉa eṉavum colluka. kaṭuvuṅ kipiḻi eṇṇeyum kāraṇam; mayir nalla āyiṉatu eṉpa ākkam.	(uka)
collumāṟu : - peṇṭāṭṭi allaḷ āṇmakaṉ eṉṟum, āṇmakaṉ allaṉ peṇṭāṭṭi eṉṟum, oṉṟaṉṟu pala eṉṟum, pala alla oṉṟu eṉṟum collupa. avar coṉmuṟi aṟiyār; ātalāṉ aḵtu uriyaṉmi aṟika. itu ammuṉṟiṭattum tuṇivu tōṉṟiyavaḻic coṉṉikaḻvu māṟu kūṟiṟṟu.		(uru)
26.	aṭiciṉi mutaleṉa muṟimūṉṟu mayaṅkāmi
	naṭipeṟ ṟiyalum vaṇṇac ciṉiccol.
e - tu. vaṇṇac ciṉic collātu aṭiyum ciṉiyum mutalum mūṉṟum aṭaṅkāmi aṭivē vaḻakkup peṟṟu naṭakkum.
aṭiyeṉpatoru poruḷatu kuṇam; ciṉi uṟuppu; mutal avviraṇṭiyum uṭiya poruḷ.
u - m : peruntalic cāttaṉ, peruntōṇ maṇṇi, neṭṭilit teṅku, ceṅkaṇ varāl, ceṅkāṉāri eṉa varum.
aṭiciṉi mutaleṉa mūṉṟum eṉṉātu muṟi eṉṟataṉāṉ am muṟiyiṉṟi naṭattalum uṭiya. avi vaḻakkiṉum kuṇam - iraṇṭaṭukki mutaloṭu varutalum ceyyuḷuḷ kuṇam iraṇṭaṭukkic ciṉiyoṭu varutalum eṉa;
avi varumāṟu:-
iḷam peruṅ kūṟṟaṉ; ciṟukaruñ cāttaṉ itu vaḻakku
ciṟupintūvi, ceṅkāṟ pēṭi itu ceyyuḷ, piṟavum aṉṉa.
mutaloṭu kuṇam iraṇṭaṭukkutal vaḻakkiyal.
ciṉiyōṭaṭukkutal ceyyuḷāṟē.        
 itu piṟa cūttiram.
ataṉāṉ av ilēcu koḷka.
27.	oruvarik kūṟum paṉmik kiḷaviyum
	oṉṟaṉik kūṟum paṉmik kiḷaviyum
	vaḻakki ṉākiya uyarcoṟ kiḷavi
	yilakkaṇa maruṅkiṟ collā ṟalla.
e - tu. uyartiṇi oruvarip paṉmiyāṟ collutalum aḵṟiṇi oṉṟaṉip paṉmiyāṟ collutalum eṉa ivi vaḻakkiṉākiṉṟakattu uyarttuc collappaṭum; ,lakkaṇa muṟimiyāṟ collumiṭattu neṟiyalla.
u - m :- oruvaṉiyum tām vantār eṉpa. oruttiyiyum tām vantār eṉpa. oṉṟaṉiyum tām vantār eṉpa. itu vaḻuvē. āyiṉum amika.
aṉṟiyum yām vantēm, nīyir vantīr, ivar vantār eṉa oruvaṉiyum oṉṟaṉiyum uyarttic colliyavāṟu tām vantār toṇṭaṉār eṉpatu uyarcol kuṟippu niliyā yiṉ, iḻipu viḷakkiṟṟu.
ilakkaṇa maruṅkiṟ collāṟalla eṉṟataṉāl uyir piṉum iḻipiṉum uvappiṉum ciṟappiṉum uyartiṇiyi? aḵṟiṇiyē pōlac collutalum aḵṟiṇiyi uyartiṇiyē pōlac collutalum vaḻakkiṉuḷ varum.
kuricil vantatu eṉpatu uyarpu poṟiyili vantatu iḻipu. oruvaṉi eṉ yāṉi vantatu eṉṟum, oruttiyi eṉ pāvi vantatu eṉṟum, ōreruttiṉi eṉ enti vantār eṉṟum, oru pacuviṉi eṉ aṉṉi vantāḷ eṉṟum collutal uvappu. cāttaṉāl vantār, nariyiṉār vantār eṉal ciṟappu. ivaṟṟil tiṇi pāl mayakkam kāṇka. 	(ue)
28	celaviṉum varaviṉum taraviṉum koṭiyiṉum
	nilipeṟan tōṉṟum annāṟ collum
	taṉmi muṉṉilip paṭarkki yeṉṉu
	mammū viṭattu muriya veṉpa.
e - tu. cellum varum tarum koṭukkum eṉa nili peṟṟup pulappaṭṭu niṉṟa annāṉku collum taṉmi muṉṉili paṭarkki eṉṉum ammūṉ ṟiṭattiṟkum uriya.
u - m :- muṉṉark kāṭṭutum.
29.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	tarucol varucol āyiru kiḷaviyum
	taṉmi muṉṉili yāyī riṭatta.
e - tu. u - m: - eṉakkut taruṅ kāṇam, eṉakkuvaruṅ kāṇam, niṉakkuvarum kāṇam eṉa varum.
30.	ēṉi yiraṇṭu mēṉi yiṭatta.
e - tu. cellum koṭukkum eṉpaṉa paṭarkkiyiṭattaṉa.
u - m :- avarkkuc celluṅ kāṇam. avarkkuk koṭukkuṅ kāṇam eṉa varum.
annāṟ collum taṉmi - muṉṉili paṭarkki - eṉṉum ammūviṭattum uriya veṉpa eṉṟataṉāṉ avi mayaṅkivaravum peṟum.
“tūṇṭil vēṭṭuvaṉ vāṅka vārātu
  puṉaṟaru pacuṅkāy tiṉṉataṉ ṟappal”      - eṉavum,
“arimala rāyntakaṇ ṇammā kaṭici
  tirumukamun tiṅkaḷuñ cettut - terumantu
  viyattum vāṉattuñ cellā taṇaṅkāki
  yiyattu niṉṟa tarā.”
eṉavum mayaṅkuvaṉa amikka. kaṭici! eṉa muṉṉili ākkuka.
31.	yāteva ṉeṉṉu māyiru kiḷaviyum
	aṟiyāp poruḷvayiṟ ceṟiyat tōṉṟum.
e - tu. yātu evaṉ eṉṉum iraṇṭu collum taṉṉāl aṟiyappaṭāta poruṭkaṇ viṉāvāyt tōṉṟum.
u - m :- nunnāṭi yātu? ippaṇṭiyuḷḷatu evaṉ? maṭiyuḷḷatu yātu? maṭiyuḷḷatu evaṉ? kiyuḷḷatu yātu? kiyuḷḷatu evaṉ? eṉavarum. iccoṟkup poruḷ yātu iccoṟkup poruḷ evaṉ eṉavum, evaṉ eṉpatu ikkālattuḷ eṉ eṉṟum eṉṉiyeṉṟum tirintu naṭakkum. ceṟiya eṉṟataṉāl yāvaṉ. yāvaṉ, yāvar, yātu, yāvi eṉavum, yāṅku, yāṇṭu, epporuḷ eṉa iviyum tiṇipāl mutaliyaṉavum aṟiyappaṭātaṉa avviṭattut tōṉṟum eṉpatu koḷka.
32.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	yāteṉa varūum viṉāviṉ kiḷavi
	aṟinta poruḷvayi ṉiyan tīrtaṟkut
	terinta kiḷavi yātalu murittē.
e - tu. yātu eṉṉum viṉāccol aṟiyāpporuḷi viṉāvutalēyaṉṟi aṟinta poruṭkaṇ aiyantīrtaṟku ārāynta collātalum urittu.
u - m :- nam erutu aintiṉuḷ keṭṭa erutu yātu eṉavarum.
terinta kiḷavi yātalumurittu eṉṟataṉāl namaruḷ yāvaṉ pōyiṉāṉ eṉa aintiṉōṭum oṭṭuka. kaṭi collilli eṉpataṉāl namaruḷ evaṉ pōyiṉāṉ. avaṟṟuḷ evverutu keṭṭatu eṉpaṉavum koḷka. (ṅu)
33.	iṉitteṉa vaṟinta ciṉimutaṟ kiḷavikku
	viṉippaṭu tokutiyi ṉummi vēṇṭum.
40.	cuṭṭumuta lākiya kāraṇak kiḷaviyuñ
	cuṭṭuppeya riyaṟkiyiṟ ceṟiyat tōṉṟum.
e - tu. cuṭṭuppeyar iyaṟpeyariṉ piṉ kiḷakkappaṭṭatu pōlac cuṭṭu mutalākiya kāraṇac colliyum iyaṟ peyariṉ piṉvittuc colluka.
u - m :- cāttaṉ kiyeḻutumāṟu vallaṉ ataṉāṟ ṟamāciriyaṉ uvakkum cātti cāntarikkumāṟu vallaḷ ataṉāṟ koṇṭāṉ uvakkum eṉa varum. cuṭṭu mutalākiya kāraṇak kiḷavi; itu viṉikkoruṅkiyalum vaḻi varum eṉṟu koḷḷuka.
41.	ciṟappi ṉākiya peyarnilik kiḷavikku
	miyaṟpeyark kiḷavi muṟpaṭak kiḷavār.
e - tu. ciṟappiṉākiya peyarkku muṉ iyaṟpeyari vittuc collār. ummiyāl tavaṅ kalvi kuṭippiṟappu mutaliyaṉavum koḷka.
u - m :- 
ēṉāti nallutaṭaṉ; vāyilāṉ cāttaṉ; paṭit talivaṉ tīraṉ; cēramāṉ cēralātaṉ; cōḻaṉ nalaṅkiḷḷi; pāṇṭiyaṉ māṟaṉ; muṉivaṉ akattiyaṉ; teyvap pulavaṉ tiruvaḷḷuvaṉ; pārppāṉ kaṇṇaṉ; vēḷāḷaṉ koṟṟaṉ; vaṇṇāṉ cāttaṉ; kōlikaṉ cāttāṉ; taccaṉ koṟṟaṉ; kollaṉ ceyyāṉ; tāvitaṉ māṟaṉ eṉa varum.	(caka)
42.	oruporuḷ kuṟitta vēṟupeyark kiḷavi
	toḻil vēṟu kiḷappiṉ oṉṟiṭaṉ ilavē.
e - tu. oru poruḷ karutivanta pala coṟkaḷukku oru toḻilē muṭipākak kūṟātu peyar tōṟum vēṟutoḻil koṭuppiṉ oru poruḷavāy oṉṟutal ilavām.
u - m :- āciriyaṉ pērūrkiḻāṉ ceyiṟṟiyaṉ iḷaṅkaṇṇaṉ cāttaṉ vantāṉ eṉṟu colluka.
iṉi āciriyaṉ vantāṉ, pērūrkiḻāṉiruntāṉ, ceyiṟṟiyaṉ kiṭantāṉ, kaṇṇaṉ pōṉāṉ, cāttaṉiṉṟāṉ eṉṉaṟka. aṉṟiyum āciriyaṉ vantāṉ, pērūr kiḻāṉ vantāṉ eṉappeyar tōṟum oruviṉi koṭuppiṉam vēṟu vēṟu poruḷām.
iṉiyittaṉmiyaṉa koḷka
enti varuka emmāṉ varuka
mintaṉ varuka maṇāḷaṉ varuka
pintoṭip paṇit tōṭkiḻavaṉ varuka.
eṉak kātalpaṟṟip palakāl oru toḻil vantaṉa koḷka.
aṉṟiyum iṉit toḻil vēṟu kiḷappi ṉoṉṟiṭa ṉilavē eṉpataṉāl vēṟu toḻil koṭātu oru toḻilē koṭuppiṉ oṉṟiṭaṉām eṉap peyar tōṟum oru toḻil koṭuttut tutiyiṭattuc colluka.
u - m :-
	tēmala raṅkaṭ ṭiruvē pukutuka
	māmalark kaṇṇi maṇāḷaṉ putukuka
	kāmaṉ pukutuka kāḷi pukutuka
	nāma veḻilviñci nampi pukutuka.
eṉpaṉa koḷka.
43.	taṉmic collē aḵṟiṇik kiḷavi yeṉ
	ṟeṇṇu vaḻi maruṅkiṉ viravutal variyār.
e - tu :- taṉmic collum aḵṟiṇic collum avi eṇṇum iṭattu virāy varutali nīkkār.
u - m :-
	“yāṉum eṉeḵkamuñ cāṟum
	avaṉuṭiya yāṉikkuñ cēṉikkum pōr 
eṉavarum.
itu itu tiṇiyum virāyeṇṇi aḵṟiṇi uyar tiṇiyōṭu muṭintatu. eṇṇuvaḻi maruṅku eṉṟataṉāl nīyu niṉpaṭikkalamuñ cāṟir, avaṉuntaṉ kaḷiṟuñ cāṟum eṉa muṉṉiliyiṉum paṭarkkiyiṉum koḷka.
44.	orumi yeṇṇiṉ potup piripāṟ col
	orumik kallatu yeṇṇumuṟi nillātu.
e - tu:- orumi eṇṇiṉi uṇarttum oruvar eṉṉum potumiyiṉiṉṟum pirintu pāluṇarttum collākiya oruvaṉ orutti eṉṉum col orumikkallatu irumi mutaliya eṇkaḷiṉ ōṭātu.
potuvāvatu - oruvar. atu irupāṟkum potu, atu piriyākkāl oruvar, iruvar, mūvar, nālvar eṉa evvaḷavum eṇ ōṭumāṟu kāṇka. appotu pirintu oruvaṉ eṉa niṉṟa piṉ iruvaṉ mūvaṉ eṉa ākātu. orutti eṉa niṉṟapiṉ irutti mutti eṉavum ākātu. ivvāṟu kūṟiyatu ṉakaravīṟum eṇṇutaṟku iyipu iṉmiyāṉum rakarīṟu iyipuṭimi yāṉum eṉka.	(44)
45.	viyaṅkō ḷeṇṇuppeyar tiṇiviravu variyār.
e - tu:- viyaṅkōḷoṭu toṭarum eṇṇuppeyar iru tiṇiyum virāy varutali nīkkār.
u - m :
	āvum āyaṉuñ celka; yāṉiyum pākaṉum varuka.
eṉavarum
ummi - eṇṇummi.	(45)
46.	vēṟuviṉip potuccol oruviṉi kiḷavār.
e - tu. vevvēṟu viṉiyiyuṭiya palaporuṭkup potuvākiya colli yātāṉum oruviṉiyāṟ kūṟār. oru viṉiyāṟ kūṟār. eṉavē ellāvaṟṟiyum uḷḷaṭakki niṟkum potuviṉiyāṟ kūṟuvar eṉpatām.
u - m :-
aṭicil eṉpatu uṇpaṉa, tiṉpaṉa, nakkuvaṉa, parukuvaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
aṇi eṉpatu kaṭṭuvaṉa, kavippaṉa, ceṟippaṉa, pūṇpaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
iyam eṉpatu koṭṭuvaṉa, ūtuvaṉa, eḻūuvaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
paṭi eṉpatu eyvaṉa, eṟivaṉa, veṭṭuvaṉa, kuttuvaṉa vaṟṟiṟkum potu.
ātaliṉ aṭicil uṇṭār, aṭicil kitoṭṭār eṉavum, aṇi aṇintār, meyppaṭuttār eṉavum, iyam iyampiṉār, paṭuttār eṉavum, paṭi vaḻaṅkiṉār, toṭṭār eṉavum potu viṉiyāṟ kūṟuka. allatu aṭicil kuṭittār, parukiṉār eṉṟum, aṇikavittār, kaṭṭiṉār eṉṟum, iyamkoṭṭiṉār; ūtiṉār eṉṟum, paṭi eytār kuttiṉār eṉṟum oṉṟaṉ toḻilāṟ kūṟaṟka.
aṟucuvi yaṭici laṇiyiḻi tarutaliṉ
uṟuvayi ṟāra vōmpātu tiṉṟēṉ
eṉa vēṟu viṉiyāl vantatāl eṉiṉ iḻittaṟkaṇ vantatu eṉka.	(46)
mūrkkaṉum mutaliyuṅ koṇṭatu viṭār
naṅkiyuṅ kiḷiyum uṇṭār.
vēntaṉ perumpati maṇṇāḷ mākka
ḷīṅkivar mūva ritaṟkuri yōrē.
vaṇṭama rōti nīyu nalli.
taṇṭār mārpi ṉivaṉum nallaṉ
olippāṭṭiṉiv vūru naṉṟē.
tīyōr yāvaru milli.
eṉa ivi tiṇivirāy eṇṇi uyartiṇiyāṉ muṭintavāṟu kāṇka.	(51)
52.	viṉivēṟu paṭūum palaporu ḷorucol
	viṉivēṟu paṭāap palaporu ḷorucoleṉ
	ṟāyiru vakiya palaporu ḷorucol.
e - tu : -  viṉivēṟupaṭum palaporuḷ oru collum viṉivēṟupaṭāp pala poruḷ oru collum eṉa iraṇṭu vakippaṭum pala poruḷ oru col.
u - m :-
avi - mā, vāḷ, kōl kaṉṟu, taḷir, pū, kāy, paḻam eṉṉum toṭakkattaṉa.
53.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	viṉivēṟu paṭūum palaporu ḷorucol
	vēṟupaṭu viṉiyiṉum iṉattiṉamum cārpiṉum
	tēṟat tōṉṟum poruṭeri niliyē.
e - tu :- viṉivēṟu paṭūum pala poruḷ oru colli aṟiyum - iṭattu vēṟupaṭṭa viṉiyiṉāṉum iṉattiṉāṉum cārpiṉāṉum poruḷteri nili teḷiyat tōṉṟum.
u - m :-
mā taḷirttatu, pūttatu, kāyttatu, eṉṟa iṭattu māmarattiṉ mēlum, māvōṭu māp pāyum eṉṟa iṭattu vilaṅkiṉ mēlum cellum. (ivi) viṉiyāṉ aṟiyap paṭṭaṉa.
iṉi, māvum mariyum pulampaṭarntaṉa; māvum nāyum ōṭiṉa eṉṟāl iṉattāl aṟiyappaṭṭaṉa.
māvummarutum ōṅkiṉa eṉṟāl ituvum iṉattāl aṟiyappaṭṭatu.
viṟpaṟṟi niṉṟu kōṟā eṉṟa iṭattuk kaṇikkōṉ mēlum, kutiriyēṟi yiruntu kōṟā eṉṟa iṭattu mattikik kōṉ mēlum palaki paṟṟi niṉṟu vāṭā eṉṟa iṭattu kaṇṭaka vāṇ mēlum cellum ivi cārpālaṟiyappaṭṭaṉa eṉakkoḷka (53)
54.	oṉṟuviṉi maruṅki ṉoṉṟit tōṉṟum
e - tu:- mēṟcoṉṉa mūṉṟu vakiyāṉum aṉṟip potuviṉiyālum viṉi poruntum iṭattiṟ poruntit tōṉṟuvaṉavum uḷa.
māvīḻntatu eṉṟa iṭattu iṉṉamā eṉṟu aṟiyalākātu vīḻtaṟṟoḻil ellāvaṟṟiṟkum potuvē yāyiṉum iviṭattu ikkālattu ivaṉ collukiṉṟatu immāviṉi eṉa oṉṟiṉi uṇartti niṉṟavāṟu kāṇka.
itaṉ karuttu:- viṉi vēṟupaṭum pala poruḷ oru col oru kāl viṉi vēṟupaṭāp pala poruḷ oru coṟ pōlavum niṟkum eṉpatu.
55.	viṉivēṟu paṭāap palaporu ḷorucol
	niṉiyuṅ kālik kiḷantāṅ kiyalum.
e - tu:- viṉivēṟupaṭāp palaporuḷ oru col ārāyum kālattu itu eṉak kiḷakkappaṭṭa ārāycciyuṭiya iṭattu naṭakkum.
u - m:-
pala kaṉṟu ōriṭattu uḷvaḻik kaṉṟu nīruṭṭuka eṉṟakkāl ellāvaṟṟiṟkum nīrūṭṭum viṉip potuvākalāṉ kaṉṟu nīrūṭṭuka eṉṟu potuvākac collātu āṉ kaṉṟi nīrūṭṭuka.
erumik kaṉṟi nīrūṭṭuka eṉak kiḷantu colluka. marak kaṉṟu pala niṉṟuḻiyum iṉṉa kaṉṟukku nīrūṭṭuka eṉak kiḷantu kūṟuka.
niṉiyuṅkāli eṉṟataṉāl ārāycciyillāta iṭattuk kiḷavātu colluka.
u - m :-
kaṉṟāṟṟup paṭutta puṉṟalic ciṟāar eṉak kiḷavātu kūṟiyavāṟu kāṇka.
56.	kuṟittōṉ kūṟṟam terittu moḻikiḷavi.
e - tu:- tāṉ oru poruḷik karutic collumēl ataṉi iṟṟeṉṟu terittuc colluka.
u - m :-
	aritārac cāntaṅ kalantatu pōla
	urukeḻut tōṉṟi varumē - murukuṟaḻum
	aṉpaṉ malip peyta nīr eṉat terittu moḻi.
	kalantatu pōla varumē yilaṅkaruvi
	yampaṉ malippeyta nīr eṉṟu kūṟaṟka.
aṉṟiyum,
	pallārtōḷ tōyntu varutalāṟ pāypuṉal
	nalvaya lūratiṉ ṟār pulāl-pullerukka
	mākiṉ maṇippūṇem mintaṉ malintamiyāṟ
	kātaṟṟāy nāṟum emakku 
eṉavum terittu moḻintavāṟu.
	ōllēṅ kuvaḷi pulāal makaṉmārpiṟ
	pullerukkaṅ kaṇṇi naṟitu
eṉṟu kūṟaṟka; kūṟil vaḻuvām. (rukū)
57.	kuṭimi āṇmi iḷami mūppē
	aṭimi vaṉmi varuntē kuḻuvē
	peṇmi aracē makavē kuḻavi
	taṉmi tiripeyar uṟuppiṉ kiḷavi
	kātal ciṟappē ceṟaṟcol viṟaṟcoleṉ
	ṟāvaṟu mūṉṟum uḷappaṭat tokii
	aṉṉa piṟavum avaṟṟoṭu civaṇi
	muṉṉattiṉ uṇarum kiḷavi ellām
	uyartiṇi maruṅkiṉ niliyiṉa āyiṉum
	aḵṟiyi maruṅkiṟ kiḷantāṅ kiyalum.
e - tu:- kuṭimi mutal viṟaṟcol īṟākac collap paṭṭa patiṉeṭṭum uṭpaṭa avipōlvaṉa piṟavum avaṟṟoṭu tokkuk kuṟippiṉāṉ uyartiṇip poruḷi uṇarttum coṟkaḷ ellām uyartiṇi muppāṟ kaṇṇum nilipeṟṟaṉa. āyiṉum aḵṟiṇip poruḷi uṇartti niṉṟuḻippōla aḵṟiṇi muṭipē koḷḷum.
u - m :-
	ivarkkuk kuṭimi naṉṟu, kuṭimi tītu,
	ivarkku eṉpataṉi yāṇṭum oṭṭik koḷka.
	āṇmi naṉṟu - tītu;
	iḷami naṉṟu - tītu; mūppu naṉṟu - tītu,
	aṭimi naṉṟu - tītu; vaṉmi naṉṟu - tītu;
	viruntu vantatu - pōyiṟṟu; kuḻu naṉṟu, pirintatu;
	peṇmi naṉṟu - tītu,
	aracu vantatu - pōyiṟṟu; maka naṉṟu - tītu;
	kuḻavi aḻutatu - kiṭantatu
ulakamum uyirum uṭampum makkaṭ paṇpu; allaṉa teyvam eṉak koḷka.
pāl pirinticiyā vuyartiṇi mēṉa; uyartiṇimēṟ pāl pirinticiyā eṉka.
59.	niṉṟāṅ kicittal ivaṇiyal piṉṟē.
e - tu:- īṟu tiriyātu niṉṟāṅku niṉṟu uyartiṇiyāy icittal kālam ulakam mutaliya coṟkaḷiṭattu iyal pilli; eṉavē kuṭimi mutaliya coṟkaḷiṭattu niṉṟāṅ kicittal iyalpām eṉpatām.
kālamulakam eṉṉum cūttiram.
u - m :-
	kuṭimi nallaṉ; āṇmi nallaṉ;
	iḷami nallaṉ; mūpput tīyaṉ eṉa oṭṭikkoḷka.
ic cūttiram kuṭimi yāṇmi eṉṉum cūttirattukkup puṟanaṭi eṉṟaṟika.
60.	icittalum uriya vēṟiṭat tāṉa.
e - tu:- kālam mutaliya coṟkaḷ uyartiṇiyāy icittalum uriya; īṟu tirintu vāypāṭu vēṟupaṭṭaviṭattu, vēṟiṭamāvatu īṟutirital.
kālam eṉpatu makaravīṟu, atu kālaṉ eṉat tirintatu. piṟavum ēṟkumāṟu tirittuc colluka.
u - m :-
kālaṉ koṇṭāṉ; ulakāppaṉ, uyirkkiḻavaṉ pōyiṉāṉ
eṉa oṭṭuka.
ic cūttiram kālamulakam eṉpataṟkup puṟanaṭi. 	(60)
61.	eṭutta moḻiiṉañ ceppalum urittē.
e - tu:- itu aruttāpatti kūṟukiṉṟatu. iṉamākiya poruṭkaṇ oṉṟi vitantu kūṟiyavaḻi accol taṉakku iṉamākiya piṟaporuḷik kuṟippāṉ uṇarttalum urittu.
eṭutta moḻi-vitanta col.
u - m :-
aṟañ ceytāṉ tuṟakkam pukum eṉṟa vaḻi maṟañ ceytāṉ niriyam pukum eṉpatu uṇarttiṟṟu. iḻipaṟin tuṇpāṉ iṉpam eytum eṉak kaḻipēririyāṉ iṉpam eytāṉ eṉpatu uṇarttiṟṟu. kuṭaṅ koṇṭāṉ vīḻntāṉ eṉak kuṭam vīḻntatu collāmal muṭintatu.
piṟavum aṉṉa.
iṉañ ceppalu murittē eṉṉum ummiyāṟ ceppāmiyum urittu. ā vāḻka. antaṇar vāḻka. eṉṟāl oḻintaṉavum oḻintārum cāka eṉpatu aṉṟu eṉak koḷka.
62.	kaṇṇum tōḷum muliyum piṟavum
	paṉmi cuṭṭiya ciṉinilik kiḷavi
	paṉmi kūṟuṅ kaṭappā ṭilavē
	tamviṉik kiyalum eḻuttalaṅ kaṭiyē.
e - tu :- kaṇṇum tōḷum muliyum avi pōlvaṉa piṟavum paṉmiyik kuṟittu niṉṟa ciṉi nilimiyi uṇarttiya coṟkaḷ tamakku uriya paṉmi viṉikku ēṟṟa akara īṟṟāṟ kūṟak karutātu, tammutal viṉikku ēṟṟa orumi īṟṟāṉumpaṉmi īṟṟāṉum kūṟak karutiya vaḻit tamakku uriya paṉmiyāṟ kūṟappaṭum muṟimiyuṭiya alla.
u - m :-
kaṇnallaṉ, kaṇ nallaḷ, kaṇnallar;
tōḷnallaṉ, tōḷ nallaḷ, tōḷnallar;
mulinallaṉ (?) mulinallaḷ, muli nallar eṉavum
kaṇ nontāṉ, muli nontāṉ eṉavum varum.
piṟavum eṉṟataṉāṟ puruvam kātu kāl mutaliyaṉa koḷka.
paṉmi kūṟuṅ kaṭappāṭila
eṉṟataṉāl orumikkum koḷka.
mūkku nallaḷ, koppūḻ nallaḷ eṉka. tamviṉikkiyalu meḻuttalaṅkaṭiyē, eṉṟataṉāl tam mutalukku iyalum eḻuttāṉ muṭiyum eṉpatām.
ic cūttiram aḵṟiṇi uyartiṇiyāṉ muṭiyumāṟu kūṟit tiṇivaḻuvamittatu.
taṉṉiṉa muṭittal eṉpataṉāṟ kōṭukūriyatu. kuḷampu kūritu, kutiri eṉat tiṇivaḻuviṉṟi aḵṟiṇip paṉmic ciṉippeyar niṉṟu mutal viṉiyākiya orumiyāṉ muṭivaṉavum koḷka.	(62)
mutalāvatu kiḷaviyākka utāraṇam muṟṟum.


iraṇṭāvatu vēṟṟumi iyal
63.	vēṟṟumi tāmē ēḻeṉa moḻipa.
e - tu :- vēṟṟumi eṉṟu collappaṭuvaṉa ēḻu vakiya.
vēṟṟumiyāvatu coṟkaḷi vēṟṟumi ceyvatu. ēḻuvakiyāvatu; cāttaṉuṇṭāṉ eṉṟa iṭattu eḻuvāyum, cāttaṉ cōṟṟi uṇṭāṉ eṉṟa iṭattu iraṇṭāvatum, cāttaṉ koṟṟaṉoṭu vantāṉ eṉa mūṉṟāvatum, cāttaṟkuk koṭuttāṉ eṉa nālāvatum, cāttaṉiṉ valiyaṉ eṉṟa iṭattu aintāvatum, cāttaṉatāṭi eṉṟa iṭattu āṟāvatum, cāttaṉiruntāṉ muṟṟattukkaṇ eṉa ēḻāvatum ām.
ippaṭiyāka vēṟṟumippaṭu poruḷ koṇṭu varutalāl vēṟṟumi ēḻeṉappaṭṭatu.	(ka)
64.	viḷikoḷ vataṉkaṇ viḷiyō ṭeṭṭē.
e - tu:- viḷi ēṟkum peyaroṭu vēṟṟumi eṭṭām. viḷikoḷvataṉkaṇ eṉṟataṉāl viḷi elātaṉavum uḷa. avi viḷimarapiṟ kāṇka.
65.	avi tām.
	peyar ai ōṭu ku
	iṉ atu kaṇ viḷi eṉṉu mīṟṟa.
e - tu :- eṭṭu vēṟṟumiyum viḷi vēṟṟumiyi iṟutiyāka uṭiya peyarum, aiyum, ōṭuvum, kuvvum, iṉṉum, atuvum, kaṇṇum, ām.
66.	avaṟṟuḷ,
	eḻuvāy vēṟṟumi peyar tōṉṟu niliyē.
e - tu :- eḻuvāy vēṟṟumi urupum viḷiyum elātu, peyarākavē tōṉṟu nilimi yatuvām.
u - m :-
ā eṉa varum.
67.	poruṇmi cuṭṭal viyaṅkoḷa varutal
	viṉinili yurittal viṉāviṟ kēṟṟal
	paṇpukoḷa varutal peyarkoḷa varutaleṉ
	ṟaṉṟi vaṉittum peyarppaya ṉiliyē.
e - tu:- eḻuvāy vēṟṟumi poruṇmi cuṭṭal mutalākiya āṟuvakippaṭṭa payaṉiliyiyum ēṟkum.
u - m :-
poruṇmi cuṭṭal:- āvuṇṭu eṉpatu,
viyaṅkoḷa varutal :- ā celka eṉpatu,
viṉiniliyurittal :- ākiṭantatu eṉpatu,
viṉāviṟ kēṟṟal :- āvō eṉpatu,
paṇpukoḷa varutal :- ākaritu eṉpatu,
peyar koḷa varutal :- ā oṉṟu, pala eṉpatu.
poruḷ eṉṉātu poruṇmi eṉṟataṉāl ā - eṉṉum poruḷ, keṭṭatēṉum av āviṉatu cātittaṉmi ek kālattum keṭātu niṟkum eṉpatu koḷka.
68.	peyariṉākiya tokiyumā ruḷavē
	avvu muriya appā lāṉa.
e - tu :- peyarum peyarum tokka tokiyumuḷa aviyum uriya av eḻuvāy vēṟṟumiyāyp payaṉili kōṭaṟku.
u - m :-
yāṉikkōṭu uṇṭu-celka. vīḻntatu, kōṭō, paritu, pala
eṉa varum.
ummiyāl viṉiyoṭu peyartokkatūum eḻuvāy vēṟṟumiyāyp payaṉili koḷḷum.
kolyāṉi uṇṭu - celka eṉa oṭṭikkoḷka. aṉṟiyum, tokiyu māruḷavē eṉṟataṉāl aṟuvakip paṭṭa tokic collum eḻuvāy vēṟṟumiyāyp payaṉili koḷḷum eṉka.
u - m:-
yāṉik kōṭu kiṭantatu, matimukam viyarttatu. kol yāṉi niṉṟatu, karuṅ kutiri yōṭiṟṟu, uvāppatiṉāṉku kaḻintatu, poṟṟoṭi vantāḷ eṉa varum.
69.	evvayiṟ peyarum veḷippaṭat tōṉṟi
	avviyal niliyal cevvi teṉpa.
e - tu :- taṉmi muṉṉili paṭarkki eṉṉum mūṉṟiṭattup peyarum viḷaṅkat tōṉṟi eḻuvāy vēṟṟumiyāyp payaṉili kōṭal cevvitu eṉpa. muṉ eḻuvāy vēṟṟumip peyar iṉṉatu eṉṟu varintatu iṉmiyiṉ ammūṉṟiṭattup peyarumē eḻuvāy vēṟṟumip peyar eṉṟu uṇarttiyavāṟu kāṇka.
70.	kūṟiya muṟiyiṉ urupunili tiriyā
	tīṟu peyarkkākum iyaṟkiya eṉpa.
e - tu :- mēl ai, ōṭu, ku, iṉ, atu kaṇ eṉṟu kūṟiya muṟiyiyuṭiya urupukaḷ tattam nili tiriyātu peyarkku īṟām iyalpi uṭiya eṉpa.
u - m :-
cāttaṉi, cāttaṉoṭu, cāttaṟku, cāttaṉiṉ cāttaṉatu, cāttaṉ kaṇ eṉa varum.
71.	peyarnilik kiḷavi kālan tōṉṟā
	toḻi ṉili yoṭṭum oṉṟalaṅ kaṭiyē.
e - tu :- peyaratu niliyilē niṟṟaluṭiya col, kālam tōṉṟātu, oru poruḷatu puṭi peyarcciyāyk kālam tōṉṟutaṟkup poruntum toḻiṟ peyar allāta iṭattu. oṭṭum - poruntum.
u - m :-
cāttaṉ eṉṟatu kālam tōṉṟātu, uṇṭāṉ eṉak kālam tōṉṟi niṉṟatu.
toḻil peyar - iṉi ōṭṭu moṉṟalaṅ kaṭiyē eṉṟataṉāl toḻiṟ peyarāy niṟkum collum kālam tōṉṟātaṉavum uḷa.
avi uṇṭal, tiṉṟal, pūcal, vēṭṭi eṉpaṉa. avi toḻilatu puṭi peyarcci māttiram uṇarttuvatallatu kālam tōṉṟā.
iṉi ellāt toḻiṟ peyarum kālam tōṉṟum eṉpatu paṭac collutal kuṟṟam.
iṉi uṇṭāṉ tiṉṟāṉ eṉpuḻit toḻiṉili oṭṭuvaṉa kālam tōṉṟi niṟṟalum kaṇṭukoḷka.
72.	iraṇṭākuvatē.
	aiyeṉap peyariya vēṟṟumik kiḷavi
	evvaḻi variṉum viṉiyē viṉikkuṟip
	pavviru mutaliṟ ṟōṉṟu matuvē.
e - tu:- peyar, ai, oṭu, ku eṉṉum cūttirattu ai eṉap peyar peṟṟa vēṟṟumic col iraṇṭāvatām. aḵti yāṇṭu variṉum viṉiyum viṉikkuṟippumākiya iraṇṭu mutaṟ kaṇṇum tōṉṟum. avi poruḷāka eṉṟavāṟu.
u - m :-
kuṭatti vaṉintāṉ, kuḻiyiyuṭiyaṉ eṉa varum
peyariya eṉpatu peyareṉpatu mutaṉiliyāka muṭintatōr peyareccam. taṉmi uṇarttuvaṉavum peyar eṉappaṭutaliṉ ai eṉap peyariya eṉṟār.
viṉiyē viṉikkuṟippeṉṟārāyiṉum avaṟṟiṉ ceyappaṭuperuḷē koḷḷappaṭum.
ceyappaṭuporuḷāvatu oruviṉi ceyyum toḻi liṉi uṟuvatu.
avviru mutaliṟṟōṉṟum eṉṟa ataṉāṉ mutalātaṟkum vēṟṟumip poruḷātaṟkum ēṟpaṉa aviyē yākaliṉ, mutal kāraṇam, avi mutalātaṟku ēṟṟavāṟu eṉṉi eṉiṉ, ceyappaṭuporuṇ mutalāyiṉa toḻiṟkuk kāraṇamākalāṉ; āyeṭṭeṉpa toḻiṉ mutaṉiliyē eṉṉum avaṟṟi mutaṉili eṉṟu kūṟupa ākalāṉ; aḵtēṟ ceyappaṭu poruḷāvatu toḻiṟpayaṉuṟuvatākalāṉ kuḻi mutalāyiṉavaṟṟiṟku av ilakkaṇam ēlāmiyiṟ ceyappaṭu poruḷ aṉmiyāṉ viṉikkuṟippu eṉal vēṇṭā; eṉiṉ, aṟṟaṉṟu; ammukkālamuṅ kuṟippoṭu koḷḷu meynniliyuṭiya eṉṟār ākaliṟ kuṟippuc coṟ kāla moṭu tōṉṟiṟ ṟoḻiṟ col - ātalum kuḻi mutalāyiṉa toḻiṟpayaṉ uṟutalum āciriyaratu tuṇipu ātaliṉ aviyum ceyappaṭu poruḷām eṉpatu, āyiṉ - ceyap paṭuporuṭ ṭōṉṟum eṉa amiyum; viṉiyē viṉik kuṟippu eṉal vēṇṭā eṉiṉ, terinili viṉic ceyappaṭu poruḷiyē kūṟiṉārō kuṟippu viṉic ceyappaṭu poruḷum aṭaṅkap potu vakiyāṟ kūṟiṉārō eṉṟu aiyam akalāmiyiṉ viṉiyē viṉik kuṟippu eṉal vēṇṭum eṉpatu.
viṉi, viṉik kuṟippu eṉpaṉa īṇṭu āku peyar. evvaḻi variṉum eṉṟataṉāl, pukaḻi niṟuttāṉ, pukaḻi niṟuttal, pukaḻiyuṭiyaṉ, pukaḻi uṭimivatu peyaroṭu toṭarnta vaḻiyum viṉic ceyappaṭu poruḷum kuṟippuc ceyappaṭu poruḷum paṟṟiyē niṟkum.
ceyappaṭu poruḷ, iyaṟṟappaṭuvatum vēṟupaṭukkap paṭuvatum eytappaṭuvatum eṉa mūṉṟām.
iyaṟṟappaṭutalāvatu muṉ illataṉi uṇṭākkuvatu. vēṟu paṭuttal muṉ uḷḷataṉit tirittal, eytappaṭuvatu toḻiṟ payaṉuṟun tuṇiyāy niṟṟal.
ivvurupi muṭittaṟku, mēṟkāppu mutaliya vāyppāṭu paṟṟi varum poruḷkaḷi im mūṉṟu kūṟṟāṉum pakukkiṉṟār, viṉimāttiriyum viṉip peyarumākap pakuppar.
īṇṭu kuṭatti vaṉintāṉ eṉṟalum mēl varum iḻi eṉṉum iyaṟṟappaṭum poruḷi.
atu eyili iḻittāṉ eṉa varum.
eṉṟatu eyili yiḻittalic ceytāṉ eṉṉum poruṭṭu, iḻi eṉṉum viṉi māttiri uṇarttum peyar nili, muṉṉal iḻittal eṉṉum peyarit tōṟṟuvittu; iḻittali eṉa urupiyum ēṟpittuc ceytāṉ eṉa viṉi yoḻinta kāraṇaṅkaḷ ēḻāṉum nikaḻum kāriyattiṉiyum tōṟṟuvittu niṟṟaliṉ iḻi eṉṉum mutaṉilip peyari vēṟṟumip poruḷāka eṭuttu ōtiṉār. ip poruṭkaṭ ṭōṉṟiya ceytāṉ eṉṉum kāriyattuṭa ṉallatu, eyili eṉṉum iraṇṭāvatu muṭiyāmi uṇarka.
ivvāṟē mēl ic cūttirattil oḻinta poruḷ kaḷiyum virikka.
iṉi viṉik kuṟippiṟkum kuḻiyiyuṭiyaṉ eṉpuḻi uṭiyam eṉak karutal viṉi. ak karutti nikaḻttukiṉṟāṉ viṉi mutal. ak kuḻi avaṉ karuttu nikaḻttappaṭu poruḷāyk kiṭakkiṉṟa taṉmi; ceyappaṭu poruḷ. ivvāṟē oḻintaṉavum kuṟippāṟ kāṇka.	(ya)
73.	kāppi ṉoppi ṉūrtiyi ṉiḻiyi
	ṉōppiṟ pukaḻiṟ paḻiyi ṉeṉṟā
	peṟali ṉiḻaviṟ kātaliṉ vekuḷiyiṟ
	ceṟali ṉuvattaliṟ kaṟpi ṉeṉṟā
	aṟuttaliṟ kuṟittaliṟ ṟokuttaliṟ pirittali
	ṉiṟuttali ṉaḷavi ṉeṇṇi ṉeṉṟā
	vākkaliṟ cārtaliṟ celaviṟ kaṉṟali
	ṉōkkali ṉañcaliṟ citippi ṉeṉṟā
	vaṉṉavum piṟavu mammutaṟ poruḷi
	leṉṉa kiḷaviyu mataṉpāla veṉmaṉār.
e - tu :- ceyappaṭu poruṇ mūṉṟaṉiyum paṟṟivarum vāypāṭukaḷi virikkiṉṟār.
kāppu mutalākac citippu īṟākac collappaṭṭa irupatteṭṭup poruḷum avi pōlvaṉa piṟavumākiya ac ceyappaṭuporuṇ mēl varum ellāc collum av iraṇṭām vēṟṟumip pāla eṉṟu colluvar pulavar.
eṉṟā eṇṇiṭic col. iṉ ellām puṇariya ṉili yiṭip poruḷiṇilikkutalāl eṇṇiṉkaṇ vantaṉa. am mutal eṉpatu mēṟ kūṟappaṭṭa ceyappaṭu poruḷ.
u - m :-
eyili yiḻittāṉ eṉa iyaṟṟappaṭuvatu iḵtu oru taṉmittu ākaliṉ oruvāypāṭē kūṟiṉār.
kiḷiyi ōppum, poruḷi iḻakkum ivaṟṟiṟku vēṟupāṭu:- ōr toḻiluṟuvikkappaṭṭut tāṉē pōtalum, toḻiṟ payaṉuṟṟa māttiriyāy oruvaṉ koṇṭupōtalum.
nāṇayaṟukkum marattik kuṟikkum. ivaṟṟiṟku vēṟupāṭu ciṟitiḻavāmal vēṟupaṭuttalum ciṟitiḻakka vēṟu paṭuttalum perumiyic curukkutalum,
nellit tokukkum, vēliyip pirikkum. ivaṟṟiṟku vēṟupāṭu virintatu tokuttalum tokuttatu virittalum,
aṟatti ākkum, nāṭṭic citikkum. ivaṟṟiṟku vēṟupāṭu mikuttalum keṭuttalum,
iv veṭṭum vēṟupaṭukkap paṭuvaṉa.
ūrik kākkum, eṉ tantiyi okkum, tēri ūrum, kurucilippukaḻum, nāṭṭip paḻikkum, putalvaṉip peṟum. maṉiviyik kātalikkum, pakivari vekuḷum, ceṟṟāric ceṟum, naṭṭāri uvakkum, nūlik kaṟkum, poṉṉi niṟukkum, ariciyi aḷakkum, aṭikkāyi eṇṇum, ūric cārum, neṟiyic cellum, cūtiṉik kaṉṟum, karuṇiyi nōkkum, kaḷḷari añcum eṉap pattoṉpatum eytappaṭuvaṉa. ivitām toḻiluṟuvaṉavum toḻiṟ payaṉuṟuvaṉavumāy varum vēṟupāṭum uṇarka.
vekuṭalum ceṟalum kolipporuḷāmvaḻi vēṟu paṭakkappaṭuvaṉavām.
ceṟal vekuḷi yatu kāriyam, uvattal kātalatu kāriyam.
vēṟu paṭukkap paṭuvatum eytappaṭuvatum pal ilak kaṇatta ātaliṉ, palavāy pāṭṭāṉ virittuk kūṟiṉār.
aṉṉa piṟavum eṉṟataṉāṉ pakivarip paṇintāṉ, cōṟṟi aṭṭāṉ, kuḻiyi uṭiyaṉ, poruḷi ilaṉ eṉṉum toṭakkattaṉa koḷka.
iyaṟṟap paṭutaṉ mutalākiya vēṟupāṭu kuṟippuviṉic ceyappaṭu poruṭkaṇ ēṟpaṉa koḷka.	(yaka)
74.	mūṉṟākuvatē,
	ōṭuveṉap peyariya vēṟṟumik kiḷavi
	viṉimutal karuvi yaṉimutaṟ ṟatuvē.
e - tu :- mēl ōṭu eṉappeyar koṭuttu ōtiya vēṟṟumic col mūṉṟāvatām. atu viṉimutalum karuviyumākiya iraṇṭu kāraṇattiyum poruḷāka uṭittu. mēl ataṉiṉiyaṟal mutaliyaṉa āṉ urupiṟku ēṟpa uṭampoṭu puṇarttuc cūttiram ceytaliṉ avaṟṟiṟku muṉṉark koṇṭa ōṭu eṉpataṟkum āṉ eṉpataṟkum viṉi mutal karuvi koḷka.
āṉ ālāyt tirintu niṟkum.
viṉi mutal karuttā eṉṟalum oṉṟu.
karuttā niṉṟu taṉṉi oḻinta karuvi mutalākiya kāraṇaṅkaḷ ēḻaṉiyum kāriyattiṉ kaṇ nikaḻttuvatu, viṉi mutal :-
atu iyaṟṟum viṉimutalum ēvum viṉi mutalum eṉa iru vakippaṭum.
u - m :-
koṭuyoṭu tuvakkuṇṭāṉ eṉpatu iyaṟṟum viṉi mutal. atu koṭi taṉṉoṭu tuṭakkutalic ceyyap paṭṭāṉ cāttaṉ eṉṉum poruṭṭu, koṭiyiṉatu nekiḻcci īṇṭu karuvi; koṭi cāttaṉit toḻiluṟuvikkuṅkāl tāṉ avaṉi nīṅkā uṭaṉikaḻcciyi viḷakkiṟṟu ōṭu eṉurupu.
aracaṉāṉ iyaṟṟappaṭṭa tēvakulam eṉpatu viṉi mutal.
iṉik karuvi kāraṇam ētu nimittam eṉpaṉa tammuḷ vēṟupāṭuṭiya vēṉum ottaporuṭṭu. akkāraṇam mutaṟkāraṇamum tuṇik kāraṇamum eṉa iruvakippaṭum.
mutaṟkāraṇamāvatu:- kāriyattoṭu oṟṟumiyuṭiyatu.
tuṇikkāraṇamāvatu (am mutal.) am mutaṟ kāraṇattiṟku tuṇiyākiya viṉi mutaliya eṭṭum, karuviyāvatu viṉi mutaṟ ṟoḻiṟ payaṉic ceyappaṭu poruṭkaṇ uyppatu, atuvum iyaṟṟutaṟkuk karuviyākiya kārakak karuviyum ñāpakak karuviyum eṉa iruvakippaṭum ūciyoṭu kuyiṉṟa tūcum paṭṭum eṉpatu kārakak karuvi. atu ūci koṇṭu cāttaṉ kuyilutalic ceyyappaṭṭa tūcum paṭṭum eṉṉum poruṭṭu; cāttaṉ ṟoḻiṟ payaṉi ūci tūcilum paṭṭilum nikaḻttiṟṟu.
maṇṇāṉ iyaṉṟa kuṭam eṉpatu mutaṟ kāraṇam, ituvum kārakak karuvi. uṇarviṉāṉ uṇarātāṉ, pukiyiṉāl vari uḷḷatu uṇarntāṉ, ivi ñāpakak karuvi.
ivaṟṟiṟku uṇarvu mutaṟ kāraṇam,
kārakamāvatu:- toḻili iyaṟṟuvippatu,
ñāpakamāvatu aṟivippatu.
iṉi, āṉ :- akattiyaṉāṟ ṟamiḻurikkappaṭṭatu. vēlāṉ eṟintāṉ eṉa varum.
viṉi mutal karuvikkaṇ ōṭu eṉurupu ikkālattu arukiyallatu vārātu.
ōṭu, ulakattō ṭoṭṭa oḻukal.
kāvōṭaṟak kuḷantoṭṭāṉum
nāvīṟṟirunta pulamā makaḷōṭu naṉpoṉ
pūviṟṟirunta eṉavum.	(ku)
75.	ataṉi ṉiyaṟa lataṟṟaku kiḷavi
	ataṉviṉip paṭuta lataṉi ṉātal
	ataṉiṟ kōṭa lataṉoṭu mayaṅkal
	ataṉo ṭiyinta voruviṉik kiḷavi
	ataṉo ṭiyinta vēṟuviṉik kiḷavi
	ataṉo ṭiyinta voppa loppuri
	iṉṉā ṉētu vīṅkeṉa varūum
	aṉṉa piṟavu mataṉpāla veṉmaṉār
e - tu :- ataṉiṉiyaṟal :- maṇṇāṉiyaṉṟakuṭam.
ataṟṟaku kiḷavi :- vāyāṟṟakkatuvāycci, aṟivā ṉaminta cāṉṟār, ivi karuvip pāṟpaṭum.
ataṉ viṉippaṭutal :- nāyāṟ kōṭpaṭṭāṉ, cāttaṉāṉ muṭiyum.
ik karumam ivi viṉi mutaṟpāṟpaṭum.
ataṉiṉātal eṉpatu :- vāṇikattāṉāyiṉāṉ.
ataṉiṟ kōṭal :- kāṇattāṟ koṇṭa arici,
iviyum karuvip pāṟpaṭum,
ivaṟṟiyum uriya pōlak kūṟiṉār.
ataṉoṭu mayaṅkal :- eṇṇoṭu virāya arici
ataṉoṭiyinta oruviṉik kiḷavi:- āciriyaṉoṭu vantāṉ māṇākkaṉ; varutaṟṟoḻiliruvarkkum ottaliṉ oruviṉik kiḷavi āyiṟṟu.
ataṉoṭiyinta vēṟu viṉikkiḷavi :- maliyoṭu poruta māli yāṉi. malikku viṉi iṉmiyiṉ itu vēṟuviṉik kiḷavi āyiṟṟu.
ataṉoṭiyinta ‘oppaloppuri :- poṉṉoṭirum paṉiyar, niṉṉoṭu piṟar, oppillataṉi oppākak kūṟutaliṉ oppaloppuri āyiṟṟu.
in nāṉkaṟkum uṭaṉikaḻtal potu eṉpatūum avi oṭu eṉum urupiṟkē uriya eṉpatūum uṇarttiya ataṉōṭiyital nāṉkaṟkum kūṟiṉār.
iṉṉāṉētu :- iṉṉumāṉu mētuviṉ kaṇ varum, muyaṟciyiṟ piṟattalāṉ oli niliyātu, tavattāṉ peṟṟāṉ vīṭu eṉka.
ataṉiṉātal kāraka ētu muṉ kūṟappaṭṭamiyāṉ itu ñāpaka ētuvām.
aintāvataṟkuriya iṉṉurupu īṇṭuc colliyatu iṉṉum āṉum ētup poruṇmikku uriyaṉa eṉṟu variyaṟi ceytaṟporuṭṭu. iṉṉāṉ eṉpatu ummit toki.
iṉṉāṉ eṉpatu :- kaṇṇāṟkotti avaṉ, kālāṉ muṭavaṉavaṉ eṉṟumām.
ciṉi viṉimutaṟkēṟiyavāṟu.
eṉa eṉpataṉi māṟṟi ētu eṉpataṉ piṉ koṭuttu iṅku varūu maṉṉa piṟavum eṉa amikka.
uṟaḻ maṇiyā ṉuyarmaruppiṉa
peṇṭakiyāṟ pēramarkkaṭṭu
ivi āṉ oṭu urupu āyiṟṟu.
maṉattoṭu vāymi moḻiyil, itu oṭu āṉāyiṟtu. aṉṉa piṟavum eṉṟataṉāṉ tūṅku kiyā ṉōṅku naṭiya.
matiyō ṭokku mukam, cūloṭu kaḻuti pārañ cumantatu.
itu kaṭpulaṉākā oru viṉi oṭu eṉṟaṟika.	(kaṅa)
76.	nāṉkākuvatē,
	ku eṉap peyariya vēṟṟumik kiḷavi
	yepporu ḷāyiṉuṅ koḷḷumaṟ ṟatuvē.
e - tu :- mēl ku eṉap peyar koṭuttu ōtappaṭṭa vēṟṟumik kiḷavi nāṉkāvatām. aḵtu yātāṉum oru poruḷāyiṉum ataṉittāṉ ēṟṟu niṟkum eṉṟavāṟu.
u - m :-
antaṇarkku āvik koṭuttāṉ.
eṉa varum.
māṇākkaṟku nūṟporuḷi urittāṉ eṉak koṭip poruḷavākiya collāṉ aṉṟip piṟavāy pāṭṭāṟ kūṟappaṭuvaṉavum māṇākkaṟku aṟivu koṭuttāṉ eṉak koṭuppāṉ poruḷāyk koḷvāṉ kaṭ cellātu āṇṭuttōṉṟum poruḷum aṭaṅkutaṟku epporuḷā yiṉum eṉṟār.
77.	ataṟkuviṉi yuṭimiyi ṉataṟkuṭam paṭutaliṉ
	ataṟkup paṭuporuḷi ṉatuvāku kiḷaviyiṉ
	ataṟkiyāp puṭimiyi ṉataṟporuṭ ṭākaliṉ
	naṭpiṟ pakiyiṟ kātaliṟ ciṟappiṉeṉ
	ṟapporuṭ kiḷaviyu mataṉpāla veṉmaṉār.
eṉṟatu :- ataṟku viṉiyuṭimiyiṉ, karumpiṟkuvēli; mayirkku eṇṇey; mēṉikku mañcaḷ; uyirkku uṇṭi eṉpaṉa. viṉi īṇṭu upakāram.
ataṟkuṭampaṭutaliṉ :- cāttaṟku makaḷ uṭampaṭṭār; cāṉṟōr kolikku uṭaṉpaṭṭār eṉpaṉa.
ataṟkup paṭuporuḷiṉ; cāttaṟkuk kūṟukoṟṟaṉ, koṟṟaṟkuk kūṟu tēvaṉ eṉpaṉa potuvākiya poruḷip pakukkuṅkāl oruvaṟkuppaṭum poruḷ eṉa aṟika.
atuvāku kiḷaviyiṉ :- kaṭi cūttiratiṟkup poṉ. poṉ kaṭi cūttiramāyt tiriyum ātaliṉ atu vāku kiḷavi eṉṟār. kiḷavi - poruḷ.
ataṟkiyāppuṭimiyiṉ : kikkiyāppuṭiyatu kaṭakam eṉpatu.
ataṟ poruṭṭākaliṉ :- kūḻiṟkuk kuṟṟēval ceyyum, varicikku uḻum eṉpaṉa.
naṭpiṉ :- avaṟku naṭṭāṉ, avaṟkuttamaṉ eṉpaṉa.
pakiyiṉ :- avaṟkup paki ; avaṟku māṟṟaṉ eṉpaṉa.
kātaliṉ :- naṭṭāṟkuk kātalaṉ putalvaṟku aṉpuṟum eṉpaṉa.
ciṟappiṉ :- vaṭukaracarkkuc ciṟantār cōḻiyavaracar, kaṟpārkku ciṟantatu cevi eṉpaṉa.
ap poruḷ eṉṟatu ataṉ poruḷ; atu aṉṉa piṟavum eṉṟavāṟām. atu ivvāṭiyum an nūlāl iyaṉṟatu eṉṟatu pōlak koḷka.
ap poruṭ kiḷaviyum eṉṟataṉāṟ piṇikku maruntu; naṭṭāṟkut tōṟṟali nāṇātōṉ; avaṟkut takkāṉ ivaṉ; uṟṟāṟkuriyar poṟṟoṭi makaḷir; iccoṟkup poruḷitu; avaṟkuc cōṟu uṇṭu; niṉakku valivāḷ; makkaṭkup paki pāmpu; ivvūrkku avvūr kātam; maṉikkuppāḻ vāṇutaliṉmi eṉpaṉa koḷka.	(karu)
78.	aintākuvatē,
	iṉṉeṉap peyariya vēṟṟumik kiḷavi
	itaṉi ṉiṟṟitu veṉṉu matuvē.
e - tu :- mēl iṉ eṉap peyar koṭuttu ōtappaṭṭa vēṟṟumik kiḷavi aintāvatām. aḵtu ip poruḷiṉ it taṉmittu ip poruḷ eṉṉum poruṇmiyi uṇarttum.
aintāvatu poruvum elliyum nīkkavum ētuvum eṉṉum nāṉku poruṇmittu.
avaṟṟuṭ poru iru vakippaṭum; uṟaḻ poruvum uvamip poruvum eṉa.
uṟaḻtal: oṉṟiṉ oṉṟi mikuttal.
ētuvum iruvakippaṭum. ñāpaka ētuvum kāraka ētuvum eṉa. avaṟṟuḷ ñāpaka ētup poruṇmi mēlē kūṟappaṭṭatu. kāraka ētup poruṇmi accam ākkam eṉpaṉavaṟṟāṟ peṟappaṭum.
nīkkap poruṇmi :- tīrtal paṟṟuviṭutal eṉpaṉavaṟṟāṟ peṟappaṭum.
ēṉi iraṇṭum itaṉiṉiṟṟitu eṉpataṉāṟ koḷḷap paṭum.
av viraṇṭiṉiyum aḵtu iru muṟiyāṉ uṇarttum ākalāṉ. ellip poruḷ - karuvūriṉ kiḻakku ivvūr, itaṉiṉ ūṅku itu eṉa varum.
kiḻakku ūṅku eṉpaṉa, avi viṉik kuṟippallavēṉum iṟṟeṉṉum poruḷpaṭa niṟṟaliṉ iṟṟu eṉṟalēyām.
porūup poruṭku utāraṇam muṉṉark kāṭṭutum. (kacu)
